  1. Just a fun collection of characters based off the SCP Universe, more top-bottom designed than bottom-top balanced. These include wacky and extraordinary concepts that push the boundaries of BOTC whilst still fitting within what makes it special.

    • The worlds of Blood on the Clocktower and the SCP Foundation have clashed in a tremendous crossover! In this collection, you'll find chaotic characters that range from simple to complex, each based off an SCP, Group of Interest, or Person of Interest from the SCP Foundation Universe! In this collection, there is a surplus of each character type present that make use of the SCP Universe's lore:

    • Townsfolk take up arms and utilize SCP objects directly, taking the objects and tools into their hands that help contribute to the forces of good. Through the use of these SCP objects they wield, they help uncover the Demon and the Minions that support them, prolong the life of their teammates, or even disrupt evil activity, with such items as instant-cure pills (SCP-500), infinitely bouncy rubber balls (SCP-018), desire-reading cameras (SCP-978), and stones that enable travel to different worlds (SCP-093).

    • Outsiders are anomalous and unexplained humans (& humanoids) of the foundation, and while they are still aligned with the foundation and wish to prevent the Demon's uprising against the town, their biological or social behaviour hinders their own team unfortunately. Their effects usually sit on either disastrous while alive to dangerous while dead, including such characters as the unwilling dimension-hopper (SCP-507), the clown who forces people to laugh (SCP-1799), or the guy... with a fish head (SCP-527).

    • Minions are composed of dangerous SCPs, including those that may or may not be considered sapient. The Minions in this collection have a diverse set of skills and abilities that allow them to work well in any script, despite not having much synergy individually in this. These dangerous SCPs include a moving statue (SCP-173), a misanthropic AI (SCP-079), a memetic hazard (SCP-055), light lamps that project demonic shadows (SCP-205), a stalking quadruped (SCP-372), a child-hating unbreakable pinata (SCP-956), a skeleton that rips the skins off others (SCP-3114), a hypnotizing music sheet composed of other's blood (SCP-012), a complex structure of pipes (SCP-015), a mechanically-converting virus (SCP-217), and even bullets that shoot from the sky (SCP-2578).

    • Demons have joined forces with the most dangerous SCPs to become lethal walking hazards during the night, as their powers wreck havoc against the poor citizens of the town. These Demons range from quiet and slightly game altering, to loud and completely insane to deal with, whether your on their opposing team, or not. This rogue's gallery brings in threats and monsters such as a plague doctor with a killing touch (SCP-049), a surface-phasing masochist (SCP-106), a giant immortal reptile (SCP-682), endothermic voice-stealing predators (SCP-939), a mask that possesses others (SCP-035), invisible sleep-preventing monsters (SCP-966), a teddy bear that constructs evil creations (SCP-1048), a staircase to hell (SCP-087), a horde of lawn flamingos (SCP-1507), a shadow that steals the living (SCP-017), an incurable skin disease (SCP-610), and a briefcase that holds something worse than death, but greater than life (SCP-5000).

    • Lastly, Travellers are made up of a variety of the abovementioned types of SCPs between safe and dangerous. However, none of these SCPs (nor their wielders) have any true alignment to the cause against good and evil, and thus exist in a grey area where their abilities are all over the place. They include a body-enhancing cola bottle that eventually kill it's drinker (SCP-207), a book of diseases that transmits them upon reading (SCP-1025), an amulet preserving the life of a junior research (Dr. Bright), and [DATA REDACTED] (SCP-701).

  3. Abdominal

    Good Townsfolk know you're the Abdominal. Evil wins at dusk if you're dead. You are sober & healthy. [Minimum 1 Outsider per evil]

    “I just woke up one day, and there it was. I don't have any idea how it got there.”

    The Abdominal (SCP-007) has a world revolving in his abdomen, and thus the world revolves around him.

    • All Townsfolk have knowledge of who the Abdominal is. This doesn't apply to Outsiders or the rare good Minion.
    • This does not apply evil Townsfolk however.
    • If the Abdominal is dead, the good team will lose come dusk unless they can win before then.
    • This must occur, considering the Abdominal can never be drunk or poisoned.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, replace Townsfolk character tokens with Outsider character tokens until number of Outsiders equal the number of starting evil players (or do nothing if it already equals or outnumbers it.)

    During the first night, wake a good Townsfolk. Show them the THIS PLAYER IS info token, then the Abdominal token, then point at the Abdominal player. Put the Townsfolk to sleep. Repeat this for each good Townsfolk.

    At dusk, if the Abdominal is dead, declare that the game ends and evil wins.

    Townsfolk

  4. Ball o' Death

    Twice per game, at night*, choose a player: the 1st Townsfolk, Outsider, & Minion who choose them with their ability before you next wake die then.

    “All staff are reminded again to not ‘play’ with Ball o' Death. There are disastrous consequences both during and after that action. -Scientific Director “Hex””

    The Ball o' Death (SCP-018) is a rubber ball that increases it's bounciness efficiency for each bounce. However, the velocity of the bounces within it's space can prove fatal.

    • Twice per game at night, the Ball o' Death can throw itself towards a player. The 1st of each character type that gets too close to your mark via selecting them with their ability will be hit by the ball.
    • The Demon cannot be killed this way.
    • Players hit the ball don't die immediately, but instead die from their injuries once the Ball o' Death next wakes.

    How to Run

    Each night except the first, remove any BALLIN' reminder on any player marked with it, and all players with the BOING! reminder die - replace their BOING! reminder with the DEAD reminder, then wake the Ball o' Death. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put the Ball o' Death to sleep.

    If the Ball o' Death chose a player, mark them with the BALLIN' reminder. The Ball o' Death loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (The Ball o' Death won't wake again.)

    At any time, if a player chooses to use their ability on a player with the BALLIN' reminder and there isn't a player of the same character type marked with the BOING! reminder, mark them with the BOING! reminder.

    "otherNightReminder": "All marked players die. If the Ball o' Death has not yet fully used their ability: Ball o' Death either shows the 'no' head signal, or points to a player. The 1st Townsfolk, Outsider, & Minion choosing them with an ability are marked to die when you next wake.",

    Each night except the first, remove any BALLIN' reminder on any player marked with it, and all players with the BOING! reminder die - replace their BOING! reminder with the DEAD reminder, then wake the Ball o' Death. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put the Ball o' Death to sleep.

    If the Ball o' Death chose a player, mark them with the BALLIN' reminder. The Ball o' Death loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (The Ball o' Death won't wake again.)

    At any time, if a player chooses to use their ability on a player with the BALLIN' reminder, mark them with the BOING! reminder.

  5. Contestant

    Once per day*, the Storyteller's narrations will subtly contain a hint (arbitrary without this ability.)

    “Contestants, are you ready? No? Begone then!”

    SCP-024 is a gameshow that will may supplement the game and it's contestants with useful hints to win big!!!

    • When the Storyteller publicly speaks, listen wisely; their words may contain a divination into the past, present, or future amongst their words.
    • Between each dusk, the Storyteller is obligated to subtly give a hint once per day through their narration towards the gamestate.
    • This does not occur on the 1st night or day.
    • This could during basic game events, such as dawn, dusk, and execution, or even through public consultations or public abilities.
    • Everyone knows what is said when the Storyteller publicly speaks, so when it occurs, other players besides the Contestant may also pick up on the hint.
    • The Storyteller may still be elaborate and intricate in their narrations as need be, whether or not the Contestant is present.
    • If the Contestant is not in-play, or their ability isn't functioning (drunkenness or poisoning), the Storyteller may choose to provide a faulty hint about a true or false game scenario.

    How to Run

    During the first night, place the HINTED reminder on the Contestant (no hints can be provided on the 1st night or day)

    After each dusk, remove the HINTED reminder on the Contestant.

    Once per day, you must provide a hint towards the gamestate in your narrations or public declarations before the end of the next dusk. Once you do, mark the Contestant with the HINTED reminder.

    JSON (Older)

    
  {
    "id": "contestant_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/contestant_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "reminders": [
      "Hinted"
    ],
    "name": "Contestant █",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Once per day*, the Storyteller's narrations will subtly contain a hint (arbitrary without this ability.) ",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "flavor": "Contestants, are you ready? No? Begone then!"
  }

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    While setting up the game, before deciding on what character tokens to put in the bag, publicly announce what the Contestant's █ variable will be for this game. This should be a standard unchanging number, write down the number on a piece of paper or on a phone.

    Once per day, if the Contestant requests a private chat with you, take them away from the circle so you cannot be overheard. Whisper them a question, and the possible answers (or answer format) they can respond with. (If you cannot think of a question straight away, either take your time or ask them to come back in a few minutes.) If the Contestant answers incorrectly, they lose a lifeline - place or replace any of the WRONG, WRONG 2 TIMES, or WRONG 3 TIMES reminder tokens to add up to the number of answers they've answered incorrectly. If those reminder tokens add up to the Contestant's █ variable, instead, remove them and mark their character token with the DEAD reminder. Tonight, the Contestant dies.

    
  {
    "id": "contestant_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/contestant_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Contestant answered incorrectly their █th time (which is publicly revealed before the tokens are finalized and put into the bag) today (or higher): the Contestant dies.",
    "reminders": [
      "Wrong",
      "Wrong 2 times",
      "Wrong 3 times",
      "Dead"
    ],
    "setup": true,
    "name": "Contestant █~",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Each day, visit the Storyteller to learn a question & answer it: the █th (and +) time you are wrong, you die tonight. [█ is known before setup]",
    "attribution": "Ability: @le_nuberto. Image: @le_nuberto. Help: @supergdpwyl",
    "otherNight": 18.024
  }

    Attribution

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Townsfolk

  6. Living Drawing

    Once per game, draw your ability (you learn what it is). Minions sees this drawing & if 1 publicly guesses what your ability does, your team loses.

    “𖨆”

    The Living Drawing (SCP-085) is a hand-drawn being that can interact with the images they are located in.

    • Once per game, during the day, the Living Drawing may come to the Storyteller to draw a new ability in fruition.
    • After the drawing is made, the Storyteller decides what the Living Drawing’s ability is. It may be the same ability as another character in Blood On The Clocktower, something similar, or something original.
    • The Living Drawing directly knows what their ability is once they draw it.
    • The Living Drawing may wake at any time during the night to learn information or to choose a player, or their ability may be passive - not requiring action from the player.
    • The upcoming night, once the Living Drawing has made their ability, Minions see this drawing directly.
    • If 1 then guesses what this ability is doing, then the Living Drawing's team loses.
    • The guess must be public, and is shared between all Minions.

    How to Run

    During any day, the Living Drawing can request a private chat with you. Take them away from the circle so you cannot be overheard. They will draw their ability on a piece of paper. Afterwards, decide what ability the Living Drawing has - write it on a piece of paper or on a phone, and show them. During the game, treat that player as if they had that ability, waking them when needed, prompting them to choose players when needed, or whatever else is appropriate. Use the Living Drawing’s ? reminders if you need to. Additionally, mark them with the ART COMMISSIONED reminder token.

    At night, if the Living Drawing is marked with the ART COMMISSIONED reminder token, remove it and wake each Minion. Show the Living Drawing's drawing. Put each Minion to sleep.

    At any time during the game, if a Minion publicly guesses what the Living Drawing's ability is and is incorrect, mark the Living Drawing with the GUESS USED reminder. Future guesses by Minion players have no effect.

    At any time during the game, if a Minion publicly guesses what the Living Drawing's ability is doing (or they are relatively close to this), the game ends. Declare that the evil team wins.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "livingdrawing_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/livingdrawing_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Living Drawing made their ability today: Wake all Minions, show them the 'This character selected you' card, the Living Drawing's token, and their drawing. If the Living Drawing's ability causes them to wake tonight: Wake the Living Drawing and run their ability.",
    "reminders": [
      "Art commissioned",
      "Guess used",
      "?",
      "?",
      "?"
    ],
    "name": "Living Drawing █",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Once per game, draw your ability (you learn what it is). Minions sees this drawing & if 1 publicly guesses what your ability does, your team loses.",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "otherNight": 65.085
  }

    Alternative JSON

    If you want the Living Drawing to not a possible lose condition on their team at the cost of having a less stronger ability, use this JSON. This is recommended if your group doesn't prefer win-condition effects, especially on top of a Townsfolk. 
    
  {
    "id": "livingdrawing_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/livingdrawing_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Living Drawing's ability causes them to wake tonight: Wake the Living Drawing and run their ability.",
    "reminders": [
      "?",
      "?",
      "?"
    ],
    "name": "Living Drawing █+",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Once per game, draw your ability (you learn what it is).",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "otherNight": 65.085
  }

    You can make the Living Drawing’s ability slightly better than a normal Townsfolk’s ability. Not having the ability to fully share it lest they lose the game to a Minion's guess, the player will need to work harder to reap its full benefit for its team.

    Generally, if a Minion makes a guess that is completely accurate on the Living Drawing's effects, but not when they are occurring ('Each night', 'Each day', 'If a player was executed today', etc.), that is generally close enough to be considered a valid correct guess.

    Attribution

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Townsfolk

  7. Red Sea Stone

    Between each night's end, learn & gain an off-script official Townsfolk's ability, but if an evil died previously, you are drunk.

    “מעבר לאש ותימרות העשן, כשמסתכלים על המתרחש בארץ, רואים החרבות של ערכי חיים בסיסים. למשל, לדונם אדמה ניתן ערך גבוה יותר מערך חיי האדם החיים עליו. או שמירה על מצבה המיוחסת לדמות מיתולוגית חשובה יותר משמירה על השומרים. למתבונן מרחוק זה נראה כנטול שמץ של הגיון. ואלו החיים בתוך המערבולת לא רואים את האבסורדיות שנוצרה עקב התעוותות ערכי היסוד.”

    The Red Sea Stone (SCP-093) is a stone that when pressed up against a mirror, allows for the carriers to travel into that mirror's dimension, which may hold different ways of life than our own.

    • At the end of each night, the Storyteller decides an off-script Townsfolk ability to give the Red Sea Stone.
    • They retain this ability until the next time, at which point they gain a new one as the next day begins.
    • Only officially released Townsfolk tokens are allowed for the Red Sea Stone to gain.
    • When an evil player dies at any time, the next time the Red Sea Stone would get their ability, they will be drunkened.
    • When the Red Sea Stone gets their ability while drunk or poisoned, they will still be shown what character token's ability they think they have, but they didn't, and thus will secretly be ability-less until the next time, even if made sober/healthy by other abilities in-between.

    How to Run

    At the end of each night, if the Red Sea Stone is marked with the DRUNK reminder token, remove it, else, if they are marked with the EVIL DIED reminder token, they are now drunk - replace the reminder token with the DRUNK reminder token. Prepare the character token or written ability of the off-script official Townsfolk. Swap the the Red Sea Stone/current off-script character token with the chosen character token and mark them with the IS RED SEA STONE reminder. If you don't have the off-script Townsfolk character's reminder tokens on hand, you can instead use the Red Sea Stone's ? reminder tokens. Wake the Red Sea Stone, show them this character token. Put them to sleep.

    At any time, if an evil player dies, mark the Red Sea Stone with the EVIL DIED reminder token.

    You should be giving abilities to the Red Sea Stone based off both team's state of winning or losing respectively, even under the concern that doing so would provide said the Red Sea Stone valuable information about this aspect. "You start knowing...", "Once per game...", "On your 1st day..."), if you believe neither team is winning, you will give the Red Sea Stone each night/day (including the first or not) abilities, and if you believe that the good team is winning, they will give either each night/day, or limited-use abilities, even though its likely that an evil player died since then, causing the Red Sea Stone to instead be drunk.

  8. Prognosticator

    You start knowing a prophecy. If fulfilled, a Minion dies tonight.

    “An all-star collection of the greatest super-stories ever published!”

    SCP-095 are 1932's comic books that accurately predicted the stories and events of the... fabled... Ronald Reagan?

    • The Prognosticator learns a prophecy, typically one of a disaster that the Prognosticator has some control over.
    • The Prognosticator does not learn when the prophecy is fulfilled.
    • If all Minions are dead when the Prognosticator's prophecy is fulfilled, nothing special happens.

    How to Run

    During setup, write a prophecy on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Prognosticator. Show them the prophecy, then put them to sleep.

    Once the prophecy is fulfilled by any means, the Prognosticator has activated their ability - mark them with the FULFILLED reminder token.

    Each night except the first, if the Prognosticator is marked with the FULFILLED reminder token, remove it and a living Minion dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder, then replace the FULFILLED reminder token with the NO ABILITY reminder.

  9. Multitool

    Once per game, choose to make a wish. If granted, it comes with an ironic price (you know what).

    “Hmm, what say... what say... Screwdriver? No. Jackhammer? Hmm, not that either... Rocket-shoes? Nah, too corny... A-ha, I got it! You can never go wrong with a spring-loaded boxing glove.”

    This Multitool (SCP-117) can deploy anything for use, at a terrible price.

    • The Multitool can make any wish they want, and it may be approved by the Storyteller.
    • This wish can be made privately, publicly, during the day, or during the night.
    • Once the Multitool makes their wish, they will know what exactly the wish has become.
    • This includes the upsides and downsides of said wish, but it does not need to refer to any targets.

    How to Run

    When the Multitool makes a wish, either verbally or via text, decide whether to accept or decline the wish. If the wish is declined, prompt the Multitool to wish again, or tell them that they have no more wishes.

    If the wish is granted, say “Your wish is granted.” or “Your wish is my command”, or nod, or otherwise signal that their wish is accepted. Mark them with the WISH USED reminer token, make a price: make whatever mechanical adjustments to the game you feel are necessary for the wish to be balanced, but try to theme it so that its a twist on what their wish is. Whenever you can, then signal to the Multitool what the actual effects of the wish are. Use the Multitool’s ? reminders if needed.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "multitool_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/multitool_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "firstNightReminder": "If the Multitools's wish requires actions at night, run these.",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Multitools's wish requires actions at night, run these.",
    "reminders": [
      "Wish used",
      "?",
      "?",
      "?"
    ],
    "name": "Multitool █",
    "team": "townsfolk",

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want to use the older version of the Multitool, whereby it was more limited and then died after using their abilities for some time, use this JSON. This version of the character is depreciated, due to it being an easy role-cop that could also kill the Demon during the night.

    During each night, if it's a night after Day 3, the Multitool will die - mark them with the DEAD reminder. If they live through it or the Multitool wasn't going to die, wake the Multitool. They point to a player and shows the Multitool fingers (1, 2, or 3) representing which ability they want to use on their target, but the 3rd option cannot be used on the first night. If the Multitool raised one finger, give a thumbs up or a thumbs down. If the Multitool raised two fingers, mark the chosen player with the SAFE reminder - the players marked SAFE cannot die until dusk. If the Multitool raised three fingers and it isn't the first night, that player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder, then the Multitool loses their ability to kill - mark them with the NO KILLING reminder. Put the Multitool to sleep.

    At dusk, remove the SAFE reminder if it's present.

  10. Living Gun

    Good players you publicly refer to might die or be executed today, even if for some reason they could not.

    “Oh! Did you hear that? Sounded like death! Someone died... Something to that effect. Tend to hear that a lot around here. Now that I think about it... The whole place just... echoes with death!”

    The Living Gun (SCP-127) is an ordinary looking gun that is actually biological internally. The bullets it fires are made from the teeth it grew to life.

    • The Living Gun has a heavy trigger finger, and it shoots to kill any names.
    • Should the Living Gun name any good player publicly during the day, they might die or be executed that same day.
    • Kills and executions do not occur during the night however.
    • The Living Gun kills good players regardless of their character type - including good Minions and possible good Demons.
    • Good players that the Living Gun refers to without directly naming are also candidates for being killed (as long as it wasn't private), such as by pointing.
    • Lastly, it is possible for the Living Gun to execute and kill concurrently against the same player.

    How to Run

    Each time the Living Gun publicly names a good player, put the NAMED reminder by their character token. At any time that day, you may decide that they die. Immediately declare that they died if so. Each dusk, remove all NAMED reminder tokens.

    JSON

    "reminders": [
      "Named",
      "Named",
      "Named"

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want the Living Gun to its older version where it instead recharged abilities, use this JSON.

    During the day, the Living Gun can declare that they wish to use their ability. If so, the Living Gun points at any player. If the chosen player has a limited-use ability, restore it by acting as though it had not been used before - remove any 2 USES, 1 USE, ABILITY USED, and NO ABILITY reminder tokens on their character token, and re-allow them to wake up. Additionally, re-enable abilities that act on a character's first night in existence. Use the the Living Gun’s ? reminders if needed, to substitute for the character’s own reminders. If the chosen player does not have a limited-use ability, nothing happens. Either way, the Living Gun loses their ability - put the Living Gun's 1 USE reminder token by their character token, or the NO ABILITY reminder token if it's already present.

  11. Eyepod

    Each night, choose a player: when you next wake, learn if they were chosen by an ability. This info is arbitrary if a Minion was chosen. [+0 or +1 Eyepod]

    “They’re all-seeing but see nothing but us. Isn’t that funny? -Junior Researcher Jefferson”

    The Eyepod (SCP-131) are bio-mechanical pet-like organisms that only want to lolly-gag around and entertain their visual stimuli.

    • Each night, the Eyepod stalks a player and lookouts for any witnesses.
    • When they next wake, they'll learn if another player choose them with an ability in-between then and now.
    • This information is arbitrary if you chose a Minion - you may learn either "yes" or "no" by the Storyteller's discretion.
    • The Eyepod will not be considered to have chosen themselves for their ability.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting the character tokens in the bag, you may choose to replace one Townsfolk token with a Eyepod token.

    While preparing the first night, if there is a 2nd Eyepod, put the Eyepod's IS #2 reminder token by that player.

    During each night, wake any Eyepods. If any player is marked with the respective CHOSEN reminder, nod your head 'yes', or nod your head any manner if they're a Minion. If any player is marked with the respective LOOKOUT reminder, nod your head 'no', or nod your head any manner if they're a Minion. Remove their respective LOOKOUT and CHOSEN tokens from the player marked with it. The Eyepod points to a player. Mark them with the LOOKOUT reminder. Put that Eyepod to sleep. Repeat until all Eyepods have acted.

    At any time, if a player chooses to use their ability on a player with the respective LOOKOUT reminder, replace it with the respective CHOSEN reminder.

  12. "3-D" Specs

    You start knowing if an even number of evil players are on the half opposite from you.

    “Not everything needs to be three-dimensional, especially when the third dimension is so violent.”

    These "3-D" Specs (SCP-178) allow the wearer to see extraordinary creatures in their viewpoint.

    • On the first night, the "3-D" Specs learns if there are an even number of evil players on the other half of the circle opposite of them.
    • The Storyteller will clarify which players are considered to be on the half opposite of the "3-D" Specs player.
    • They learn this only once and then learn nothing more.

    How to Run

    While preparing the first night, put the "3-D" Specs's KNOWS LEFT and KNOWS RIGHT reminder tokens by the two players to respectively indicate that them and the players in-between are considered to be part of the half opposite to the "3-D" Specs player.

    During the first night, wake the "3-D" Specs, point to the players marked KNOWS LEFT and KNOW RIGHT, and drag your finger across all players in between, nod your head 'yes' if there are an even number of evil players in there, and nod your head 'no' for otherwise.

  13. Sauelsuesor

    If you nominated a player today (not yourself), tonight, learn if they are a Minion, and if so, what Minion they are.

    “I am too big, and you are too small. There is a sea of nothing and islands of light. I am their shore. To you come the monsters. The pounding fists of void. The longing gods beyond our knowledge. I am the lookout. I see the ripples in their wake. You want me to pledge my sight-know to you, only to you, so you, only you, can be greater. Even if you find, restrain, defend. You want me to be yours. That is not why I am here. There are others. Others I assist. Others I warn. Others beyond your thin walls of grey, dry paste-rock. Others beyond the reach of your weary satellites. Others beyond the home, our home. Others I know. Others I love. Others you won't care for. Others that came before. And, over all, others beyond the little walls of rules and bone and laws and flesh and memories and oaths you build around yourselves until you don't even remember them. Others I love. Dearly. And yet, only my brother is an equal to me.”

    The Sauelsuesor (SCP-179) is an astral lookout who willingly protects humanity from celestial threats.

    • Each night, the Sauelsuesor learns either a "yes" and a character, or a "no".
    • This does not occur if they didn't nominate anyone but themselves.

    How to Run

    Each day, if the Sauelsuesor makes a nomination on another player, mark the nominee with the STARGAZED reminder.

    Each night except the first, if the STARGAZED reminder is present on any player, wake the Sauelsuesor. If the player marked STARGAZED is a Minion, shake your head yes and show their character token. If this is not the case, nod your head no. Then, remove the STARGAZED reminder and put the Sauelsuesor to sleep.

    "otherNight": 77.179

    Alternative JSON & How to Run

    If you feel like you want to buff the Sauelsuesor by being able to find any evil player at the cost of learning their exact character, use this JSON. This is recommended if you don't have many other evil finding sources on your script.

    Each day, if the Sauelsuesor makes a nomination on another player, mark the nominee with the STARGAZED reminder.

    Each night except the first, if the STARGAZED reminder is present on any player, wake the Sauelsuesor. If the player marked STARGAZED is an evil player, shake your head yes. If this is not the case, nod your head no. Then, remove the STARGAZED reminder and put the Sauelsuesor to sleep.

  14. Architect

    Each night, choose 2 players (not yourself): tonight, abilities choosing the 1st instead go the 2nd unless the game would end.

    “Let’s ensure Site-19 never sees this thing, that’d be the disaster to cause the most logical XK-Class. -Security Lt.“Messer””

    The Architect (SCP-184) expands any in-order space it's place in into non-euclidean environments that distort the space around them.

    • Each night, the Architect will choose 2 players, and distort the space around them.
    • That night, if a player chooses the 1st chosen player with their ability, they are secretly redirected into choosing the 2nd instead.
    • If said action would cause the game to immediately end, such as by killing the Demon, instead, no change occurs.
    • Actions that would lead to the game ending eventually such as by the death of a Klutz are unaffected however.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the Architect. They point at any two players, one at a time. Put the Architect to sleep. Mark the 1st chosen player with the IN-PORTAL reminder, and the 2nd chosen player with the OUT-PORTAL reminder.

    At night, if a player chooses to use their ability on the player marked with the IN-PORTAL reminder, their ability is instead redirected to choose the player with the OUT-PORTAL reminder unless this would immediately result in the game ending.

    At dawn, remove the IN-PORTAL and OUT-PORTAL reminder.

  15. Tunisian

    Twice per game, choose a player (not yourself): the latest chosen is drunk if Outsider or Minion & cannot die at night if allied.

    “يبدو أنك تُعاني. استسلم، سينتهي كل شيء قريبًا!”

    SCP-244 is a Tunisian jar that constantly disperses a fog of pure cold air that cools down the body to it's core.

    • The Tunisian makes the chosen Outsiders and Minions drunk and protects the chosen allies during the night.
    • Whilst the Tunisian can make their choice at any time during the game, they also privately wake up at night to make their choices silently if they so wish.
    • Both effects can occur at once, including Outsiders but irregularly on a good Minion.
    • The Tunisian's protection for allied players is similar to the Innkeeper in which it makes players not just safe from being killed by the Demon, but also from death caused by Outsiders, Minions, Townsfolk, and Travellers.
    • The Tunisian only protects chosen allied players at night, not the day.
    • Whilst the Tunisian only has two uses of their ability, the effects they impose on their latest chosen player are not limited to just that night and upcoming day.
    • If the latest chosen player changes character or alignment, the effects imposed by the Tunisian will update accordingly.

    How to Run

    At any time during the game, the Tunisian may choose a player, either publicly or by having a private discussion with you. Each night, wake the Tunisian in-case they want to make their choice during the night privately. They either shake their head no or point at any player except themselves (If the Tunisian points at themselves, shake your head no and prompt them to point at another player.) Put the Tunisian to sleep.

    If the Tunisian chose a player, mark them with the CHOSEN reminder. If a player was already marked with the CHOSEN reminder token, instead move it onto the new player chosen. The Tunisian loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the ABILITY USED reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (the Tunisian won't wake again.)

    Players marked with the Tunisian's CHOSEN reminder token on the same team as them cannot die at night, and Outsiders or Minions marked with it are drunk.

  16. Neglected

    Each night, choose a player (not yourself): the 1st other player choosing them with their ability tonight is drunk until dusk.

    “Now you see me...”

    SCP-268 is a threaded cap that causes the wearer to be in-perceivable the longer they don it on their head.

    • Each night, the first time a player wakes to use their ability and chooses the player the Neglected, chose that player becomes drunk immediately. Their ability does not work tonight, nor the next day.
    • Later on the same night, if another player wakes to use their ability and chooses the player the Neglected chose, their ability works as normal.
    • The Neglected cannot make a player drunk unless the player chose the player they chose.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the Neglected. The Neglected points at any player except themselves. Put the Neglected to sleep. Put the Neglected's INVISIBLE reminder token by the chosen player's character token.

    At night, if a player chooses to use their ability on the player marked with the INVISIBLE reminder token, that player immediately becomes drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder and remove the INVISIBLE reminder token. Finish resolving this player’s ability and put them to sleep.

    At dawn, remove the INVISIBLE reminder (if it's present).

    At dusk, remove the DRUNK reminder (if it's present).

  17. Panacea

    Twice per game, at night, choose a player: they are safe from everything until dusk.

    “A panacea is something we’re in dire need of. Find out how to make more of these immediately. -O5-4”

    The Panacea (SCP-500) is a limited-quantity "cure-all" pill - and when it means "cure-all", it really, really means cure-all.

    • Twice per game at night, the Panacea can choose to make someone immensely better.
    • The Panacea may even choose themselves if they so wish.
    • The chosen patient will be safe from death, drunkenness, poisoning, having their information being overwritten, being redirected onto another target with their ability, being registered as evil to certain abilities (context-sensitive), being converted to the evil team, and all other sorts of negative actions or effects.
    • Despite the power and versatility this protection brings as a whole, it still won't be able to protect against Assassin-like kills.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the Panacea. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put the Panacea to sleep.

    If the Panacea chose a player, put the Panacea's ULTRA-SAFE reminder token by the chosen player's character token.

    If any player attacks, drunkens, poisons, overwrites info of, imposes a negative effect, redirects the target(s) of, or would register as evil to certain abilities (context-sensitive), converts the alignment of, or does any negative action/effect to the player marked ULTRA-SAFE, the player remains unaffected by the action. Regardless of how many actions were prevented, the Panacea loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet.

    At dusk, remove the ULTRA-SAFE reminder token.

  18. Basement Cat

    Other Townsfolk keep their ability until dusk (or think they do otherwise.)

    “Meoww meow meow meowwwwwww...”

    SCP-511 are mutant basement cats. Anomalously, their 9 lives superstition seems to manifested into reality.

    • With the Basement Cat active, the deaths of their teammate's abilities are delayed until the upcoming dusk.
    • With the Basement Cat on the script, all Townsfolk are expected to keep their ability until dusk. However, should the Basement Cat be dead, or their ability is no longer active (like through drunkenness or poisoning), they will only think they have it, similar to the Drunk.
    • This does not apply to the Basement Cat themselves.

    How to Run

    When a Townsfolk dies at night, mark them with the UNDEAD reminder token.

    Townsfolk with the UNDEAD reminder have their ability if the Basement Cat's ability is in-play & active, or think they do if not.

    Each dusk, remove all present UNDEAD reminder tokens from all players.

    
  {
    "id": "basementcat_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/basementcat_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "remindersGlobal": [
      "Undead",
      "Undead",
      "Undead",
      "Undead",
      "Undead"
    ],
    "name": "Basement Cat █",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Other Townsfolk keep their ability until dusk (or think they do otherwise.)",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "flavor": "Meoww meow meow meowwwwwww..."
  }

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    While setting up the game, before deciding on what character tokens to put in the bag, publicly announce what the Basement Cat's █ variable will be for this game. This should be a standard unchanging number, write down the number on a piece of paper or on a phone.

    While preparing the first night, put the Basement Cat's tail reminder tokens by them to indicate they have █ charges of the ability. Use the multiple 3 TAILS LEFT reminder tokens to add up to a number you can then represent with a 1 TAIL LEFT reminder token, or a 2 TAILS LEFT reminder token.

    If any non-Demon would die, they remain alive (but they die if you are drunk or poisoned). If executed, declare that the player was executed but remains alive (do not say why). Either way, a death was prevented - move around the different TAIL(S) LEFT reminder tokens to then equal the number it should be. Note that the Basement Cat counts twice, and thus you'll be performing that operation twice.

    If any non-Demon player dies for whatever reason, declare that the game ends and evil wins.

  19. Peacemonger

    Each night, choose a player. The 1st time no one died tonight*, you & all chosen teammates are safe from everything until dusk.

    “This content feeling is nice and all, but the doves might be a problem.”

    The Peacemonger (SCP-514) are doves that decay all weapons and tools of destruction within their vicinity. They are the embodiments of peace in physical form.

    • The Peacemonger will protect all chosen good players at a time of peace - when no one will die tonight from any ability.
    • The Peacemonger's ability will protect players that were chosen when they were good, not all chosen players that are good.
    • Additionally, the Peacemonger protects against the effects of anything negative, such as Poisoning and Conversion, alongside preventing death for affected players until dusk.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the Peacemonger and have them choose a player. If they choose a player on their team, put a HARMONIOUS reminder token next to that player. Put the Peacemonger to sleep.

    Each night except the first, after the Peacemonger has woken and gone to sleep, if no player is to die tonight and the Peacemonger isn't marked with the NO ABILITY reminder, all players marked with the Peacemonger's HARMONIOUS reminder token are protected from all negative effects, including death, drunkenness, poisoning, or being turned evil (see SCP-500/Panacea's how to run section to see additional actions) - put the Peacemonger's PEACETIME reminder in center of the left side of the Grimoire.

    At dusk, the Peacemonger's protection will end if it's active - remove the Peacemonger's PEACETIME reminder token, the Peacemonger loses their ability - mark them with the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (the Peacemonger won't wake again).

  20. Desire Reader

    Each day, privately choose a player: learn a trait they share with the previous player. You are sober & healthy only if they are.

    “See foe's remaining HP. Press for secrets!”

    The Desire Reader (SCP-978) is a camera that can peer into the minds of others through the photographs it makes of those targets, revealing their souls.

    • Each day, the Desire Reader chooses a player to compare with the previously chosen player.
    • They can learn any fact provided that it relates to something both players share, such as an ability trait, or what they did at night.
    • The Desire Reader learns nothing on the first day
    • It is up to the Desire Reader to talk with the Storyteller, not the other way around. This isn't a public conversation, and the group can't listen in. It's private.
    • The Desire Reader can choose not to visit the Storyteller if they wish.
    • The Desire Reader is normally sober and healthy, even if another ability targeted at the Desire Reader would make them drunk or poisoned.
    • However, if the Desire Reader chooses a drunk or poisoned player with their ability, they will be under drunk and poisoned behaviour, and will receive arbitrary information with their ability for that use.
    • The Desire Reader can choose themselves if they freely wish, and the Storyteller may give weaker information as a result.

    How to Run

    Once per day, if the Desire Reader requests a private chat with you, take them away from the circle so you cannot be overheard. They will choose a player. If there is a player marked with the PREVIOUS reminder token, whisper a fact about a commonality they share to the Desire Reader. Mark the chosen player with the PREVIOUS reminder token. Note that the Desire Reader is not affected by drunkenness and poisoning normally, but will receive arbitrary results should the player they select be, if so, mark them with the DROISONED reminder token until they choose a sober & healthy player again. If you cannot think of a thought-read straight away, either take your time or ask them to come back in a few minutes.

    JSON

    "id": "desirereader_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/desirereader_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "reminders": [
      "Droisoned",
      "Previous"
    ],
    "name": "Desire Reader █",

    Remember, the Desire Reader will be unaffected by conventional sources of drunkenness and poisoning unless their most recent choice was drunk or poisoned.   21. MAN EGG™

    Once per game, during the day, visit the Storyteller to gain the ability your team needs (learn what it is).

    “"The longer you wait, the stronger it is!™" -MAN EGG”

    The MAN EGG™ (SCP-1657) is exactly what the team needs. They're more accurate with landing a crucial ability the longer they cook and abstain from acting.

    • The Storyteller decides what the MAN EGG™'s ability is when they call for it. It may be the same ability as another character in Blood On The Clocktower, something similar, or something original.
    • The MAN EGG™ may wake at any time during the night to learn information or to choose a player, or their ability may be passive - not requiring action from them. Regardless, the MAN EGG™ knows exactly what their ability is.
    • If the MAN EGG™ used their ability while drunk or poisoned, they will still be told they got their ability, but they never actually did, and thus will be drunk/poisoned indefinitely.

    How to Run

    At any time during the day, the MAN EGG™ can approach the Storyteller if they aren't marked with the HATCHED reminder and privately to gain a new ability. The Storyteller writes down this ability on a piece of paper, or on a phone, then lets the MAN EGG™ know what it is. Mark them with the HATCHED reminder.

    During the game, treat that player as if they had that ability, waking them when needed, prompting them to choose players when needed, or whatever else is appropriate. Use the MAN EGG™’s ? reminders if you need to.

  22. Hangman

    You can't die at night. Each night, choose a unique letter: learn how many evil's names have it. If 0 for Minions, you are drunk.

    “How's it hangin'?”

    SCP-2121 is an infinitely hanging rope that allows communication with divine forces to learn answers, at the risk of the user's own life.

    • The Hangman has already been hanged... so to speak. They cannot die at night, but may are still vulnerable during the day.
    • Each night, the Hangman takes a shot at a alphabetical letter, and learns how many evil players have at-least 1 of it in their character's names.
    • The Hangman is not allowed to pick a symbol, number, or repeat letter (unless the Storyteller decides otherwise for the first two this game.)
    • If the Hangman's result is 0 specifically for Minions, the Hangman henceforth is drunk, and will receive arbitrary results and can die at night.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the Hangman. They point at a letter on the script, or otherwise indicate a letter to the Storyteller silently. If 0 evil Minions have this letter in their name, the Hangman is drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder token. Then, show them fingers (0, 1, 2, etc.) equalling the number of evil players with at-least one of those letters in their name. Put the Hangman to sleep.

    If the sober Hangman would die at night, the Hangman remains alive.

  23. Revenant

    If you die at night, learn your alive resident neighbour's characters.

    “'We must declare human death a Keter SCP,' she demanded, 'and contain it at any cost.'”

    SCP-2718 is the unspeakable and chilling truth about death - there is no afterlife, nor respite from the pain. Only the constant role of witness to your own torment.

    • When the Revenant dies at night, they are immediately woken on that night, and they learn their living neighbour's characters immediately.
    • The Revenant skips over learning dead neighbours, and avoids redundantly learning a Traveller's character.

    How to Run

    If the Revenant died tonight, wake them. Show the character tokens of their closest living non-Traveller players in that direction.

  24. Navigator

    Each night, choose a player (not yourself): you learn if they are a Minion. Characters marked on the script give reversed info.

    “Woah, look at that baby spin. It's... spinning really fast... ... Do you think it's going to expl-”

    The Navigator (SCP-2799) is a device created by the SCP Foundation to track and learn about uncontained SCP anomalies.

    • Each night, the Navigator chooses a player and learns if they are a Minion.
    • On the script, there can be any amount of characters that marked with the Navigator's character icon to indicate they will give reversed information to the Navigator.
    • This reversed information is normally false, but can be made true if an effect is being applied that makes the info they normally gain false.
    • It doesn't matter if these characters lose their abilities by drunkenness, poisoning, or death, or if they gain abilities of a character that are marked - it only matters that the player themselves are a marked character or not.
    • The Navigator is not limited to choosing living players only.

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on characters you want to give reversed info for to the Navigator.

    During each night, wake the Navigator. They point to a player. If the player is a Minion or a non-Minion marked on the script, nod your head yes. Otherwise, shake your head no. Put the Navigator to sleep.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "navigator_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/navigator_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "firstNightReminder": "The Navigator points to a player; give the head signal (nod yes, shake no) for whether that player is a Minion or not.",
    "otherNightReminder": "The Navigator points to a player; give the head signal (nod yes, shake no) for whether that player is a Minion or not.",
    "setup": true,
    "name": "Navigator █",
    "team": "townsfolk",
    "ability": "Each night, choose a player (not yourself): you learn if they are a Minion. Characters marked on the script give reversed info.",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "firstNight": 62.2799,
    "otherNight": 82.2799
  }

    Alternative JSON

    For if you don't plan on using the character marking mechanic for the Navigator, despite being ill-advised, use this JSON. 
    
  {
    "id": "navigator_scp_fragmented_veil",

    "otherNight": 82.2799

    It's recommended that you mark Townsfolk with a goal to bait being attacked at night, or Outsiders that don't want to die or be found out by evil players (ex. Soldier, Ravenkeeper, Sage, Choirboy, Plague Doctor, Sweetheart, Hatter, Damsel.) to balance out the Navigator's ability.

    In scripts designed for smaller games, such as Teensyville, it's better to reduce the marks to only Outsiders that fit the profile described.

    Attribution

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Townsfolk

  25. Trilḗnē

    If you die at night, choose 2 players: your chosen killer dies, even if for some reason they could not.

    “*RING-RING* Hello, operator? I think my... I- your... yourrr refrid- reg- refrigd-... err... Uh... I... Uhhhmmmmmmm... ... ...Is your oven running?”

    The Trilḗnē is the truth beyond death, the end of life's story, the get-petty-revenge-on-your-killer solution.

    • Once the Trilḗnē meets death, they have a chance to guess who their killer is. If correct, they get annihilated to death.
    • This annihilation transcends protection and "cannot die" effects, leaving the sapless victim to always be vulnerable to a horrible smite.
    • There can only be 1 killer per this interaction. If there 2 or more players who would've killed the Trilḗnē, only the player that killed first is the killer to this ability.
    • It is possible for the Trilḗnē to kill the Demon - why not?

    How to Run

    If the Trilḗnē died tonight, wake them. They point at 2 players. If the Trilḗnē chose their killer, that player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. This cannot be prevented in any way (except if the Trilḗnē doesn’t have their ability, such as if they’re drunk or poisoned). Put the Trilḗnē to sleep.

    Townsfolk

  26. Provenance

    You start knowing that 1 of 3 players is a Townsfolk. If they would die, they don't, then you're drunk.

    “I will protect for as long as you my kin. My spawn. My flame. Is that not what bonds and family are about? Is that not what life is about? Cherish everyday like it is a gift. Cherish everyday because it is my gift. You are safe here. You are safe now. Goodnight.”

    The Provenance (SCP-3668) is an ancient shield that summons the dead to defend their descendants from all harm against them.

    • During the first night, the Provenance is woken, shown three players, at-least one of which is a Townsfolk.
    • They learn this only once and then learn nothing more.
    • The first time that player would die for any reason, they remain alive. They don’t learn that this ability saved their life.
    • The Provenance can learn multiple Townsfolk, but only one of them will be protected.
    • If another character’s ability protects that player from death, the Provenance does not use their ability. Only the time that the Provenance's target would actually for realsy bona fide be dead does their ability trigger.

    How to Run

    While preparing the first night, put the Provenance's TOWNSFOLK reminder token by any Townsfolk character token, and put the Provenance's two WRONG reminder token by any other character token.

    During the first night, wake the Provenance and point to the three players marked TOWNSFOLK and WRONG. Put the Provenance to sleep. Remove the Provenance's WRONG reminder tokens when convenient.

    If the player marked with the TOWNSFOLK reminder token would die, they remain alive, but they die if the Provenance is drunk or poisoned. If that player was executed, declare that the player was executed but remains alive (do not say why).

    Either way, the Provenance becomes drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder token.

    "firstNight": 53.3668
  }

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    For if you feel like the Provenance should be more generic in which good players they can protect, use this JSON.

    While preparing the first night, put the Provenance's GOOD reminder token by any good player's token, and put the Provenance's WRONG reminder token by any other character token.

    During the first night, wake the Provenance and point to the players marked GOOD and WRONG. Put the Provenance to sleep. Remove the Provenance's WRONG reminder token when convenient.

    If the player marked with the GOOD reminder token would die, they remain alive, but they die if the Provenance is drunk or poisoned. If that player was executed, declare that the player was executed but remains alive (do not say why).

    Either way, the Provenance becomes drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder token.

  27. Skynet Agent

    Each night*, choose a living player: if the Minion marked on the script is chosen, they lose their ability & die. [That marked minion is in-play]

    “Y'know, I am quite content being a Silver Bullet hunter. It's not like its every once in a while you get to bag a Werewolf on a Christmas morning.”

    The Skynet Agent has a specific Minion in their sights, and will hunt them down to the ends of the earth.

    • The Minion can be in play without the Skynet Agent. During the setup phase, if the Skynet Agent is in play and the Minion marked on the script isn't, they instead replace another Minion. If that Minion is already in play, the Skynet Agent doesn't add a duplicate.
    • If the Skynet Agent correctly chooses the marked Minion at night, they are drunk and they die.
    • The Skynet Agent's ability will only concern the one Minion marked on the script, regardless if they are dead or alive - if the marked Minion does die by any other circumstances, another Minion won't be marked.

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on the Minion you want to be the target of the Skynet Agent they want to hunt down.

    Each night except the first, wake the Skynet Agent. They point at any player. If they choose the Minion marked on the script, that player dies and loses their ability - mark them with the ELIMINATED reminder. Put the Skynet Agent to sleep.

      "Eliminated"
    ],
    "setup": true,
    "name": "Skynet Agent █",

  28. Hammer Down

    On your 1st day, state your conditions. Whilst true, if a teammate was to die during the day, they don't & you're drunk.

    “62 alarms. All teams respond, code 3. All units BOL for 243 suspect. All units return to code 12. 243 assault on Protection Team. 10-0, begin scanning.”

    The Hammer Down is the [REDACTED] [REDACTED] ...tactical unit... [REDACTED] ...rescue operation... [REDACTED] [REDACTED] [REDACTED] ... last resort.

    • The Hammer Down tactically selects when they protect a teammate, or perhaps who they protect.
    • This does not regard by default the character type of who they protect, only that they are on the same alignment.
    • If the Hammer Down has not set down any conditions before the end of the 1st day, no one will be protected by them.
    • The Hammer Down protects against any death during the day, not just executions
    • These conditions do not have to be specific to the player that is dieing, they may relate to anything provided it is definite and non-subjective.
    • This can include use of 'and' rules, 'or' rules, and 'exclusive-or/xor' rules.
    • Being drunk or poisoned does not inhibit how the Hammer Down can set their conditions.

    How to Run

    During the first day, the Hammer Down may add any new conditions, or remove any conditions they previously set today, either publicly or by having a private discussion with you. Write the current set of conditions on a piece of paper. If prompted, show them what their current conditions are to remind them or clarify any confusion. That dusk, mark the Hammer Down with the PAST DAY 1 reminder token to indicate that have used their first day.

    During the day, if the Hammer Down's conditions are true, and a player of the same alignment as them would die, they remain alive (do not say why.) The Hammer Down becomes drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder token.

    "ability": "On your 1st day, state your conditions. Whilst true, if a teammate was to die during the day, they don't & you're drunk.",

    During the first day, the Hammer Down may add any new conditions, or remove any conditions they previously set today, either publicly or by having a private discussion with you. Write the current set of conditions on a piece of paper. If prompted, show them what their current conditions are to remind them or clarify any confusion. That dusk, mark the Hammer Down with the PAST DAY 1 reminder token to indicate that have used their first day.

    During the day, if the Hammer Down's conditions are true, and a player of the same alignment as them would die by execution, they remain alive (do not say why.) The Hammer Down becomes drunk - mark them with the DRUNK reminder token.

  29. GOC Physics

    Twice per game, at night*, you may be safe from the Demon & the 1st non-Demon to choose you until tomorrow is drunk then dies.

    “Fuckin' dieeeeee....”

    GOC Physics is the division of the SCP Foundation that investigates the physics and science of anomalies, in order to harness said power for the foundation.

    • The GOC Physics cannot die from the abilities whilst they're using their ability that night. So, if anything attacks them at night, nothing happens.
    • Whilst the GOC Physics is using their ability, the first time a player wakes to use their ability and chooses them, that player will die once the GOC Physics wakes. This excludes the Demon.
    • A protected player being marked to die the next night will die if not protected when it occurs.

    How to Run

    Each night except the first, remove the TRAP SET reminder token if it is present, and if any player is marked with the HEXED reminder, they die - mark them with the DEAD reminder. Then, wake the GOC Physics. They either shake their head no or shake their head yes. Put them to sleep.

    If the GOC Physics shook their head yes, mark them with the TRAP SET reminder token. GOC Physics loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (GOC Physics won't wake again.)

    If the GOC Physics would die at night whilst they have the TRAP SET reminder, they remain alive.

    At night, if a non-Demon player chooses to use their ability on the GOC Physics with the TRAP SET reminder and nobody is marked with the HEXED reminder, this player is drunk will die the next night - mark them with the HEXED reminder.

  30. Pinky

    Each night*, choose a player and a character: if correct, they die & you are drunk (unless the game ends.)

    “What's better than a conga line? A dead one!”

    The self-destructive Pinky explodes- ka-boom!

    • The Pinky chooses a player and a character. If that player is that character, they'll die, but the Pinky is drunk from then on, continuing to wake from then on.
    • If the Pinky's kill would result in the immediate end of the game (such as by killing the Demon), it doesn't occur, but the Pinky won't be drunk.

    How to Run

    Each night except the first, wake the Pinky. They point at any player, then point at any character icon on their character sheet. Put the Pinky to sleep. If the chosen player is that character from the chosen character icon, and the game won't end immediately as a result of this final action, they die - mark them with the DEAD reminder and then the Pinky with the DRUNK reminder.

Do not mark the Demon with it unless at-least one other marked player is evil.

  31. Premonious

    You start knowing how you'll die. If you avoid your fate, 1 player dies at any time.

    “A moment to gather my thoughts, please. ... Alright, let's do this.”

    The Premonious knows the worst knowledge any man can learn; how they will die.

    • The Premonious learns their given fate, and if they fail to oblige by it, it shoots you down and corrects your behaviour.
    • The fate given the Premonious may be as generic, specific, or even cryptic as the Storyteller wants.
    • This can include the how, why, when, who, and if playing in real life, maybe even where.
    • When this ending to your life is no longer possible, whether the conditions for it can no longer be met, or the Premonious has died outside of it, 1 player will die at any time.
    • Should there be any possible way to resurrect a Premonious as they die outside of their fated death, they are not considered as having avoided their fate. However, once that method is also gone and the Premonious' fate remains unfulfillable, then its considered as avoiding fate, and the punishment is applied.
    • The player that dies to the Premonious' circumstances can be anyone, regardless of alignment or character type, but is typically a Townsfolk or Outsider.
    • The death caused by the Premonious can occur at any time, such as day or night. It can even kill them during the night before they can be protected by another character to prevent any deaths.

    How to Run

    During setup, decide the Premonious' fate of how they die and write it on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Premonious. Show this phrase, then put them to sleep.

    When the conditions for this event are no longer possible by any means, even as the Premonious is dieing, or is dead, one player is dieing at any time - mark the Premonious with the AVOIDED FATE reminder token.

    At any time, whilst the AVOIDED FATE reminder token is active, you may decide for any player to die. When you do so, use the DEAD reminder token to remind yourself that the Premonious' ability has killed a player.

    JSON

    Read into the evil team's wants and intents when using the Premonious' kill to benefit them. Remember that the Premonious may sometimes net themselves an early advantage by learning of a particular in-play character, or even a hint into what character a player is. It is advised that you hint at the fact that evil players may have some say over how this kill is used later on should it be primed & active for use.

    Outsider

  32. World-Hopper

    On the 3rd night, the evil players become off script official characters. You learn which 1s (if alive) or 1 of 2 (if dead).

    “Yeah, so... that agent assigned to escort me to my chambers... he's uhh... kinda... gone... forever. Oops.”

    The World-Hopper (SCP-507) unwillingly teleports to another dimension, entering a new world of different threats that might not even be human.

    • The World-Hopper will "teleport" on Night 3 and change the evil team's characters, without the Demon's discretion or acceptance.
    • The World-Hopper's ability acts late at night, being after most character abilities go through, and just before dawn occurs.
    • This will occur, even if they're currently dead, but not if drunk or poisoned.
    • Evil players do not get any choice of what characters they'll be if they accept the World-Hopper's teleport, they're in for the ride, or they're not (coward).
    • The characters that evil players can become due to the World-Hopper may only be ones officially released.
    • Additionally, the characters that evil players can become do not necessarily have to be Minions & Demons exclusively, some may even be evil Townsfolk or Outsiders.
    • Once this all occurs, the World-Hopper will directly learn what the characters are, but if currently dead, they instead learn 2 characters: 1 is true, the other is false.

    How to Run

    When preparing the first night, put the World-Hopper's NIGHT 1 reminder by the World-Hopper. When preparing the second night, put the World-Hopper's NIGHT 2 reminder by the World-Hopper. When preparing the third night, put the World-Hopper's NIGHT 3 reminder by the World-Hopper.

    If the World-Hopper died due to an evil player's ability, mark them with the DISARMED reminder.

    During the night, even if the World-Hopper is dead, if the World-Hopper has the NIGHT 3 reminder, swap the character tokens of evil players you choose with new official character tokens, including off-script. Wake each evil player that changed character, show the YOU ARE info token, then their new character token, then put them to sleep. Wake the World-Hopper, show them each of evil player's character tokens (if they're dead, show them 2 instead, 1 true, and 1 false.) Put the World-Hopper to sleep.

    JSON

Wake the World-Hopper, even if they're dead. Show them the evil team's new character tokens, but if they're dead, also show a different character token before or after.",

    It's generally a good idea to pair the World-Hopper in scripts where the evil team has abilities that are limited in use or don't have as much effectiveness later in the game (Ex. Assassin, Godfather, Spy, Baron).

    When you can, try turning Minions into Townsfolk or Outsiders with abilities that can serve the evil team quite well in their own right. Whilst there aren't many in this regard, a few examples include the Philosopher, Sailor, Fisherman, and Plague Doctor.

  33. Fishhead

    On night (Max Outsiders + 1 - X), Townsfolk abilities yield false info until dusk. You're sober & healthy. [X Outsiders]

    “God hates me.”

    The Fishhead (SCP-527 - a.k.a Mr. Fish) has incurred the wrath of Yvith for their incredibly smelly & grotesque head.

    • Uhh... Fishhead... he.. uh..s..
    • He makes the Outsider count arbitrary... I gues...
    • And... uhh... also makes Townsfolk information false...
    • The more Outsiders there are... the early he... does ... it...
    • He's sober and healthy... so this can't... be... avoided... unless dead...
    • Also he can appear and modify Outsider count even in Base 0 Outsiders LMAO.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, add or remove any number of Outsider tokens. For the amount of Outsiders then in the bag, place the Y reminder on the center of the Grimoire, where Y is equal to (Number of Outsiders on the script plus One minus number of Outsiders in the bag).

    When the 1/FALSE reminder token is in the Grimoire, you must give false information whenever a Townsfolk ability prompts you to give information.

    Each dusk, subtract one from the Fishhead's reminder token count. If currently 1/FALSE, remove it entirely.

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want to use the older version of the Fishhead, where it was originally a joke of a character that took up space existing and breathing my air, use this JSON.

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, add one extra Outsider character token and remove one Townsfolk character token.

    At any time during the day, the Fishhead can approach the Storyteller privately for a fish joke that must involve at least 2 alive characters. It can feature more characters than that if you choose, regardless if they're alive, dead, or not in-play.

    Since the Fishhead can normally and absolutely bring Outsiders into a game that doesn't have them at base, be careful about running this, and assure your players if you have any intent of possibility doing this, or not.

    On hot summer days, remember to move the Fishhead away from windows, and to clean their enclosure every once in a while.

  34. Loyal Servant

    Each night, if good, 1 living Minion may 'accept' to swap characters & you become evil. At dusk, this is undone if you both live.

    “Forever Mine – S.J.W.”

    SCP-662 is a hand-bell that summons an obedient & loyal servant for whomever rung it.

    • Each night, 1 Minion will wake to decide if they'll ring the handbell, or not.
    • If they do, they'll swap characters with the Loyal Servant as the Loyal Servant becomes evil.
    • If they don't, nothing different happens.
    • The Loyal Servant does not know who rang the handbell, only their character by virtue of swapping with them.
    • Come dusk, and if the current Loyal Servant has their ability and both of the involved players live, they swap characters back as the original becomes good.

    How to Run

    During each night, if the current Loyal Servant is good, wake any living Minion. Show them the THIS CHARACTER SELECTED YOU info token, then the Loyal Servant token. That player either nods their head yes or shakes their head no. Put that player to sleep. If they shook their head no, then nothing happens.

    If they nodded their head yes, swap the character tokens of the Minion and Loyal Servant. If a player’s alignment does not match the color of their character token, turn it upside-down (you will need to do this for the current Loyal Servant). wake the then-Loyal Servant, show them the YOU ARE info token, their new character token, a thumbs-down, then put them to sleep. Mark the then-Loyal Servant with the IS SERVANT reminder token.

    Each dusk, if a Minion is marked IS SERVANT and both them and the Living Servant are alive, swap the character tokens of that Minion and the Loyal Servant and make the original Loyal Servant good. If a player’s alignment does not match the color of their character token, turn it upside-down.

  35. Shoe-In

    The 1st time the dead equal or outnumber the living, you join the losing team & learn 1 teammate.

    “Must... enter... ocean... must... enter... ocean... going for... schwim...”

    SCP-1667 is a non-physical contagion that infects footwear, driving anyone in physical contact to enter the pacific ocean, to which no one returns from.

    • The Shoe-In only activates their ability after a few nights have passed - once the dead players equal or outnumber the living.
    • In the odd event that the Shoe-In is currently evil and good is losing, they will turn good for this occasion.
    • The Shoe-In's chance to become evil only occurs once in the game, and if they don't become evil then, they don't become evil later.
    • This does not occur if the Shoe-In is dead or has died that night.
    • In the situation that the Shoe-In is drunk or poisoned, they do not change alignments, but can learn any player.

    How to Run

    When the number of dead players equals or exceeds the number of alive players and they aren't marked with the Shoe-In's HALF-WAY reminder token, mark them with the HALF-WAY reminder, and if you believe that the Shoe-In's opposing team is losing, rotate the Shoe-In’s character token - right side up for good, upside-down for evil - to match this player’s alignment. Wake the Shoe-In, give them a thumbs-up or a thumbs-down (indicating their current alignment). Point to one player on their team. Put the Shoe-In to sleep.

  36. Laughster

    If you weren't funny today, tonight, a player might die. During the day, you die if & only if you are exceptionally funny.

    “Get up, you worthless sack of shit... consider this a friendly reminder: GET WITH THE FUCKING PROGRAM.”

    The Laughster's (SCP-1799 - a.k.a Mr. Laugh) nature is to be funny, even if he doesn't want to.

    • The Laughster has a metaphorical gun pointed to their head: make people smile and laugh, or people they care about might die.
    • The Storyteller is the judge of whether the Laughster was funny or not.
    • The Laughster cannot end their own suffering by conventional methods; attempts to kill the Laughster during the day will fail, making their detrimental ability stick.
    • ... However, if the Laughster is exceptionally funny by the Storyteller's perspective, they might die right there on the spot.

    How to Run

    At any time (even at night), if you believe that the Laughster is relatively funny, then you can decide to mark them with the FUNNY reminder for as long as you need.

    Each night except the first, if the Laughster is not marked with the FUNNY reminder, you may choose any player. The chosen player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder.

    During the day, if you believe that the Laughster is exceptionally and uncontrollably funny (to a degree that even you cannot contain your own laughter on purpose), then you can decide to kill the Laughster. Immediately declare that they die. Otherwise, if the Laughster is killed outside of that during the day, they don't, even by execution.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "laughster_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/laughster_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Laughster was not funny today: Choose a player not protected from dying tonight. That player might die.",
    "reminders": [
      "Funny",
      "Dead"
    ],
    "name": "Laughster █",
    "team": "outsider",
    "ability": "If you weren't funny today, tonight, a player might die. During the day, you die if & only if you are exceptionally funny.",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "otherNight": 54.1799,
    "jinxes": [
      {
        "id": "lycanthrope",
        "reason": "The Laughster cannot die to the Lycanthrope."
      },
      {
        "id": "surgeon_the_bootleggers_anthology",
        "reason": "The Laughster cannot die to the Surgeon."
      },
      {
        "id": "heir_darkartists_stuff",

      },
      {
        "id": "corsair_hypers_hoard_of_homebrew",
        "reason": "The Laughster cannot die to the Corsair."
      }
    ]
  }

    It is best to be forgiving and treat even a slightly funny the Laughster as funny. It can be tough to be funny when one is expected to be, after all.

    Don't be tempted to not smite the Laughster during the day if you find them exceptionally funny despite the in-game circumstances. The Laughster is mostly a joke character meant to liven up games, and should be respected when it's able to create some comedic moments for players to enjoy.

    It is possible for the Laughster's ability to kill themselves, in-case they have a poor taste in comedy.

    Attribution

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Outsider

  37. Immaterial

    If anything bad happens to you, it might be on a living neighbour instead. If executed, you might live, and a player dies tonight.

    “Who? Oh right, me! Where? Oh right, here! Haha... ha... If you don't mind me asking, but when...”

    The Immaterial (SCP-2428 - a.k.a Mr. Mad) alters the perception of others on them.

    • If something bad happens to the Immaterial, such as death, drunkenness, or poisoning, it might be redirected on another good living neighbour.
    • Dead or evil neighbours will not be a potential candidate for this.
    • Occasionally, the Immaterial can survive execution if it succeeds and remain alive, at the cost of redirecting their death onto another player, whom may be of more importance.

    How to Run

    Each time the Immaterial would be effected by any bad action or ability - such as being killed, made drunk, or poisoned - you may choose to have it instead affect one of their living neighbours instead, and move the respective reminder token that signifies that status onto them.

    If the Immaterial is executed, you may choose to mark them with the DODGING reminder, and declare that player was executed but remains alive (do not say why.)

    Each night except the first, if the Immaterial is marked with the DODGING reminder, you choose any player not being protected that night. The chosen player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder, then remove the DODGING reminder from the Immaterial's character token.

    JSON
