    "id": "immaterial_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/immaterial_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the Immaterial is marked 'Dodging': Choose a player not protected from dying tonight. That player dies.",
    "reminders": [
      "Dodging",
      "Dead"
    ],
    "name": "Immaterial █",
    "team": "outsider",
    "ability": "If anything bad happens to you, it might be on a living neighbour instead. If executed, you might live, and a player dies tonight.",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "otherNight": 54.2428,
    "jinxes": [

        "id": "riot",

      {
        "id": "heir_darkartists_stuff",
        "reason": "If the Immaterial dies on a good player's stead, they might live but someone dies tonight."
      }
    ]
  }

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Outsider

  38. Wholesale

    The 1st (& only) time an evil player chooses to discard their vote token (secretly), they can make a wish, even if you're dead.

    “I looked up in the sky last night and all the stars were shining bright, I wished I may and I wished I might just have the wish I wished last night.”

    The Wholesale (SCP-2855 - a.k.a Mr. Money) can be whatever the buyer wants it to be.

    • The Wholesale allows for an impure dead to purchase them with their Obol.
    • If on the script, evil players are allowed to make a wish at the cost of discarding their vote token entirely, removing their last chance to vote while dead.
    • Vote tokens attained or used by other sources (like the Beggar) are applicable here.
    • If an evil player does discard their vote token, no one immediately learns this. They may still pretend to use a vote token despite having used it.
    • Like the Legion, if an evil player's use of their imaginary vote token causes a change in execution status, the false result is publicly declared, whilst the real result is secretly kept.
    • And like the Wizard or Puzzlemaster, an evil player's ability to disregard their vote token and make their wish has no limitations on when this occur - they can do this publicly, private, during the day, or during the night.
    • If an evil player does discard their vote token but the Wholesale ability isn't in-play, they learn nothing.
    • If an evil player would discard their vote token but another evil player had already done so, the vote token is not discard.
    • Otherwise, if the evil player successfully makes the wish using the vote token they discard, they learn they gained a wish.
    • The Storyteller may alert the player that they gained a wish, but that it is not granted for whatever reason, and that they should try again with another.
    • When an evil player makes their wish, the Storyteller may choose to interpret the wish differently in order to make it balanced for both teams.
    • However, these wishes will typically not come with any price or clue to it's nature.

    How to Run

    At any time during the game, either verbally or via text, a dead evil player may choose to secretly discard their vote token and make a wish if no evil player has the BROKE reminder token present on them, even if the Wholesale is not in-play. If so, they secretly lose a vote token - mark them with the BROKE reminder token. When running the vote tally, if the evil player pretends to use their vote token despite not having it, publicly announce the vote result as normal, but secretly keep of a record of which player is really about to die by marking them with the ABOUT TO DIE reminder, then execute them when nominations are over.

    If the Wholesale is in-play, decide whether to accept or decline the wish. If the wish is declined, prompt the evil player to wish again when they can.

    Once granted, say “Your wish is granted.” or “Your wish is my command”, or nod, or otherwise signal that their wish is accepted. Use the Wholesale’s ? reminders if needed, to substitute for the wish’s own reminders.

    JSON

    "id": "wholesale_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/wholesale_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "otherNightReminder": "If the evil player's wish requires action at night, run these.",

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want to use the older version of the Wholesale, where a player purchased for their allegiance with their ability, use this JSON.

    While preparing the first night, put the Wholesale's ODD reminder token by the Wholesale's character token, and you may choose to rotate the Wholesale's character token. Do not wake the Wholesale to let them know their current alignment.

    Each dusk, replace the Wholesale's ODD reminder token with the EVEN reminder token, and vice-versa.

    The first time you hear the Wholesale agree a request to join an asking player's alignment, rotate the Wholesale’s character token - right side up for good, upside-down for evil - to match this asking player's alignment. Mark the Wholesale with the SOLD reminder, and mark the asking player with the BUYER reminder.

    The player marked with the BUYER reminder is drunk every night if they're a Townsfolk, or they're drunk every odd-numbered night if they're a Minion.

    Remember to keep the Wholesale's ability when they die and to have their reminder tokens persist.

    Outsider

  39. C-Insurgency

    If a player causes or attempts something bad to happen to you, your alignment becomes that opposing them (unless killed).

    “Wow, thank you for attempting to discharge your weapon in front of your superior and commanding officer. I'll be sure to amend this piece of fine history on my auto-biography and on your obituary, jackass.”

    The C-Insurgency constantly defects to different sides.

    • At any time, if a player does something bad to the C-Insurgency, whether it was through their ability, someone else's ability, or by nominating them, the C-Insurgency immediately defects against their side.
    • At night, just before dawn, the C-Insurgency wakes to know what their resulting alignment is, even if they died in between the alignment change and them learning it.
    • The C-Insurgency does not change their alignment when dead.
    • The C-Insurgency will change their alignment when made drunk or poisoned, and then will remain drunk or poisoned for the specified duration.
    • The C-Insurgency will defect against a player if the attempt to do something bad to them, but it fails, such as being drunk or poisoned, or if the effects of an ability fail due to the C-Insurgency not meeting a certain condition.

    How to Run

    When the C-Insurgency is nominated, drunken-ed, poisoned, inflicted with any other negative status effect (whether it occurs or is attempted), or attempted to be killed by another player, if that player’s alignment is the same the C-Insurgency’s alignment, rotate the C-Insurgency’s character token—right side up for good, upside-down for evil—to oppose that player’s alignment. If made drunk or poisoned, you will still rotate the C-Insurgency character token to oppose that player, but any further rotations won't occur until the C-Insurgency is sober and healthy. Lastly, "attempts to be killed by another player" include kills that were prevented by being unable to die, or abilities with kills targeting the C-Insurgency that either failed due to drunk or poisoning, or by a certain condition not being met (ex. Slayer shot counts as an attempt to kill the C-Insurgency), but the C-Insurgency does not change their alignment if they are successfully killed however.

    Each night except the first, wake the C-Insurgency, even if they just died. Give them a thumbs-up or a thumbs-down (indicating their current alignment). Put the C-Insurgency to sleep.

    Outsider

  40. Duende

    You start knowing a question. Each day or night*, privately answer it: if correct, you see the Grimoire & turn evil.

    “
    
> wife pregnant for 8 months
> her water breaks, rush her to hospital
> she goes into intensive labour & passes away due to complications
> doctor hands me what came out of womb
> 2 gold Örtugs, a dead chicken, and 1 I.O.U
> fucking duendes
    ”

    The Duende scavenges for things to steal, and - if crafty - uncovers the booty of the Grimoire.

    • The Duende starts knowing a question, and nothing else. Should they correctly answer it, they gain the benefit of seeing into the Grimoire, and becoming evil.
    • Nothing bad happens to the good team should the Duende fail to answer their question at all whilst they live.
    • It is Duende's choice to answer their question, should they decide they want to remain good and not risk being evil.
    • The Duende can answer once per day, or night. They only wake at night to answer if they didn't do so during the day, and the type of question they have can permit a silent answer during the night.
    • The Duende effectively knows if they are incorrect in their answer, since they won't plunder any rewards on failure (unless drunk or poisoned).
    • Jinxes involving the Duende seeing into the Grimoire should be less severe against them.

    How to Run

    During setup, write a question on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Duende. Show this question, then put them to sleep.

    Once per day, if the Duende requests a private chat with you, take them away from the circle so you cannot be overheard. They give their answer. If incorrect, mark them with the INCORRECT reminder token.

    Each night except the first, if the Duende's question or the game's medium permits them to answer silently during the night and they are not marked with the INCORRECT reminder token, wake them. Show them the question again, then they give the signals necessary to answer it. Afterwards, put them to sleep.

    Each dawn, remove the INCORRECT reminder token if its present.

    If the Duende answers correctly, jackpot! Show them the “You Are” info token, and a thumbs down. Show them the Grimoire for as long as they need.

    During the first night, wake the Duende. Show this question, then put them to sleep.

    If the Duende answers correctly, jackpot! Replace their old character token with a Minion token, show them the “You Are” info token then the Minion character token, and give a thumbs down. Show them the Grimoire for as long as they need.

    If the Duende transforms into an evil Minion already in-play and you don't have duplicate character tokens for this Minion, you may instead flip the Duende's character token upside-down (to indicate to yourself that they are evil) and mark the current Minion with the USES MINION OF reminder, to indicate that they are effectively this Minion.   41. Living Room

    You become a Storyteller, but you're still evil. You cannot make players learn themselves or be subjective for abilities.

    “L̴͓̠̍I̴͖͐͋V̴͓͉̅E̷͓̓͌ͅ.̸̳̾̿ ̵̙̓̕L̸͉͛À̶̗̕Ü̴̙̈G̴̩̘̓̃H̵̝̗̓.̴̘̅̔ ̷͋͜L̵̪̼͂O̷̮͎̒V̵̖̉͘Ḛ̷͍͘.̴̤̠͒”

    The Living Room (SCP-002) is a biological living room that hypnotizes prey into entering the room and being devoured and regurgitated into fleshy furniture.

    • The Living Room isn't a player, and instead is another Storyteller playing a very cruel god.
    • Once the Living Room draws their character token, they immediately become a Storyteller (and remain evil).
    • The Living Room will handle all ability interactions (except for subjective abilities that are based off an individual viewpoint), and makes all decisions whilst not breaking any rules.
    • The Living Room is no longer a player despite working for the evil team - they cannot die, be targeted, vote, nominate, or register as anything, just like the regular Storyteller.
    • The Living Room is allowed to pass along information they learned from the Grimoire.
    • The Living Room is not allowed to be nominated & executed.

    How to Run

    Once the Living Room receives their character token, immediately and publicly declare that the Living Room is now in-play and which player it is - remove their seat and put them by your side. From now on, they are no longer a player that can be interacted with, but they are still on the evil team, and must work towards supporting the Demon and their team. They may see the Grimoire at any time, communicate to players at any time with their information, and they act on behalf of the Storyteller when a player performs their actions and receives their results. However, the Living Room cannot change the game's setup, break the rules of the game, or break the rules of the characters, they can only change decisions a Storyteller can make subjectively, effectively granting the evil team perfect luck. Additionally, since the Living Room is no longer a player, they cannot be targeted by character abilities, they cannot vote or nominate, and they are not considered alive for the vote requirement, exile requirement, or final day requirement. Lastly, the Living Room cannot force a player to learn themselves with their ability (Ex. Washerwoman, King, Steward), and they cannot Storytell for subjective abilities (ex. General, Fisherman) - the original Storyteller will handle those subjective abilities.

    Minion

  42. Orchestrator

    You start with a quest. If completed, you become a not-in-play Demon (all learn which) & 1 good player learns part of it.

    “Dance with me, just dance! We will sing and serenade until the stars align with our beliefs and wishes. We will wash this cursed blood away from us into this wretched parchment, and celebrate our joyous rebirth into new and free lives. Just come, take my hand. What's the worst that can happen?”

    The masterpiece... must... be *grk* finished... by... any means... po- *grk*... possible...

    • The Orchestrator starts with knowledge on how to complete their masterpiece, and their result is glorious...
    • Their quest for the ultimate music can be anything the Storyteller wants it to be, which can even include objectives that compromise their identity.
    • Additionally, the Orchestrator is unaware of how close they are too their objective - they are nearly blind in this aspect!
    • However, their reaped reward is the complete transformation into another Demon (that isn't in-play).
    • This comes with a cost however - all players learned which Demon came out of this demonic serenade.
    • The public announcement should normally be delayed until the next major public chronological event occurs, such as dawn, nominations open, or dusk.
    • Additionally, 1 good player with a musically-tuned ear will gain some knowledge on what this quest was in the first place.
    • Typically, the hint that the good player gains about the quest is about 1 to 3 words.

    How to Run

    During setup, write the quest on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Orchestrator. Show this quest, then put them to sleep.

    Track the Orchestrator's progress towards this quest. They may visit you to make guesses or your quest may wake them each night to choose a player. Regardless, as the Orchestrator makes progress, use their DO, RE, MI reminders if you need to.

    Once the Orchestrator has completed their quest in full, replace the Orchestrator token with a Demon token that already isn't in-play, changing that player's character from the Orchestrator into that Demon. Afterwards, wait a while, and then say "The Orchestrator has completed their masterpiece" or something similarly dramatic, and be sure to specify the not-in-play Demon that came out of it.

    If you are in an environment that allows you to private message people anonymously (such as online), immediately message the Orchestrator what Demon they are, and message a good player a slight portion of the Orchestrator's quest.

    If you are in an environment that doesn't allow you to privately message people anonymously (such as real life), mark the new Demon with the FASOLLATIDO reminder. That night, wake the Orchestrator-turned-Demon. Show them the YOU ARE info token, then their new Demon character token, then put them to sleep. If the quest was written on a piece of paper, find a way to obscure, cover, or limit visibility of most of the quest's text, or just write a slither of an existing part of it onto a new piece of paper. Wake a good player. Show them the remaining visible portion of the quest. Put the good player to sleep.

    Alternative JSON & How to Run

    If you feel like the Orchestrator should be a bit more quiet at the cost of some survival power on the evil team, use this JSON.

    During setup, write the quest on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Orchestrator. Show this quest, then put them to sleep.

    Track the Orchestrator's progress towards this quest. They may visit you to make guesses or your quest may wake them each night to choose a player. Regardless, as the Orchestrator makes progress, use their DO, RE, MI reminders if you need to.

    Upon completing their quest, the Orchestrator will still obtain a Demon's ability to use, but they will not be a Demon, and thus not uphold the evil team's win condition to see the Demon reach the final 2. However, the Orchestrator's is not revealed to everyone upon doing this, and the player who learns about the Orchestrator's quest does not learn what not-in-play Demon ability they get.

    Once the Orchestrator has completed their quest in full, replace the Orchestrator token with a Demon token that already isn't in-play and mark them with the IS ORCHESTRATOR reminder token, granting the Orchestrator's that Demon's ability. Do not announce this publicly.

    If you are in an environment that allows you to private message people anonymously (such as online), immediately message the Orchestrator what Demon they are, and message a good player a slight portion of the Orchestrator's quest.

    If you are in an environment that doesn't allow you to privately message people anonymously (such as real life), mark the new Demon with the FASOLLATIDO reminder. That night, wake the Orchestrator-turned-Demon. Show them the YOU ARE info token, then their new Demon character token, then put them to sleep. If the quest was written on a piece of paper, find a way to obscure, cover, or limit visibility of most of the quest's text, or just write a slither of an existing part of it onto a new piece of paper. Wake a good player. Show them the remaining visible portion of the quest. Put the good player to sleep.

    When building a script, keep in mind that the Orchestrator synergizes well with Demons that either gain information or poison players. Demons that introduce extra killings or protections are discouraged by a bit due to how hard it would be to maintain 2 Demons in the event that the Orchestrator transforms early, or the good team loses their valuable Townsfolk early on that can help bring their team to a reasonable chance of victory.

    As per deciding an Orchestrator's quest, very much try to make it so the quest cannot be completed on Day 1, either through the quest requiring extra days worth of work to complete over time, or through a hard-set restriction (which is un-recommended and should only be used as last resort.) It's recommended to keep it this way as to prevent snow-balling situations where the good team loses too much tempo through an early transformation that cannot be easily re-bound or course-corrected through the Storyteller.

    Minion

  43. Pipe Maze

    If a player is executed, it's redirected to another based on a rule (you start knowing it), if it can (unless it ends the game).

    “You will die here... this place will be your grave. You will be contained here forever... and I will live...”

    The Pipe Maze (SCP-015) is a sentient labyrinth of pipes with a mindless primitive goal to expand and conquer. The pipes also act as a network to disperse hazards and travesties.

    • The Pipe Maze starts with a rule. The rule can be of any length, but is typically a sentence long.
    • Whenever a player is executed, if the rule has a valid target, it will redirect that execution & kill to the end-point target.
    • Even if the originally executee could survive that execution, the execution and kill will process the new target, and disregard the previous one.
    • If the new execution would then result in the immediate end of the game (such as killing the Demon or the Saint), it is simply not processed and the ability doesn't activate for it.

    How to Run

    During setup, write the Pipe Maze's rule on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    During the first night, wake the Pipe Maze. Show this rule, then put them to sleep.

    If a player is executed, instead, another non-Demon player will be executed based on the Pipe Maze's rule, if applicable.

    JSON

    Minion

  44. Missing No.

    Marked abilities register falsely & yield false info (except choosing). You & the Demon are safe from marked non-Demon abilities.

    “Every school child knows that 2 plus 2 is 4. The solid mathematical certainty of numerical order and value is the basis for all logic-based systems. We know that after 2 comes 3 and after 3 comes 4. What this formula proves is that we missed a number somewhere. Imagine if all our technology was based on the belief that after 4 came 6. We simply didn't know or conceive of 5. That is, in essence, what this formula proves—we missed a number. -Physics & Mathematical Scientist Prof. Hutchinson”

    The Missing No. (SCP-033) is a mathematically forgotten number that disrupts the space of reality on whatever it is transcribed on.

    • Yes is no and no is yes in the chaotic world thrown in by the Missing No.
    • With the Missing No. in-play, abilities must register other players falsely, whilst simultaneously not being able to provide true info.
    • Minions become not Minions, good becomes evil, vice-versa, and etc.
    • Whilst any marked ability must yield false info different from what the user would truly learn, they only learn something false if the mis-registering of players would not affect the final result of the yielded info.
    • However, when marked players are other players with their ability, they are still prompted to choose exclusively they players they would normally be able to pick.
    • Nonetheless, this character's text is up to interpretation by the Storyteller, and may be subject to different iterations of being run based on intention and design. As always, if there is any confusion, the Storyteller should clarify any misunderstandings they may have set.
    • Lastly, both the Missing No. are and the Demon are safe from said marked abilities at all times - they cannot be drunkened, poisoned, killed at night, lose their ability, or otherwise be subjected to any negative effect.
    • This excludes the Demon's abilities, and Fabled abilities (dependant on which Fabled and depending on how the Storyteller wants to run the game)

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on characters whose abilities you want to be susceptible to the Missing. No's corruption.

    While preparing the first night, put the Missing No.'s SAFE reminder token by the Missing No. and the Demon.

    The Missing No's protection prevents all harmful effects of marked abilities, such as death, poisoning, losing your ability, or being transformed into a weaker character.

    Each time a player is targeted by a marked player's ability that detects or affects specific characters or players, choose which character, alignment, state, and or player that player registers as. Do whatever is appropriate, such as showing an good or evil character token, nodding, giving finger signals, or allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect good, evil, Townsfolk, Outsider, Minion, or Demon players. However, if the then yielded info from this operation is still true when the marked ability prompts you to give information, you must give false information instead.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "missingno_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/missingno_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "reminders": [
      "Safe",
      "Safe"
    ],
    "setup": true,
    "name": "Missing No. █",
    "team": "minion",
    "ability": "Marked abilities register falsely & yield false info (except choosing). You & the Demon are safe from marked non-Demon abilities.",
    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto"
  }

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want to use the older version of the Missing No., where it categorically disrupted information at an immensely wide scale, use this JSON.

    Each night, wake the Missing No.. They show any information feedback/signal (finger signals, characters on the script, other players, head nods, thumbs up or down, and any other type of ability target, or a combination of any). Give a thumbs up to acknowledge their choice and put them to sleep. Write down that information on a piece of paper, or on a phone.

    When a Townsfolk or Outsider would get that information with their ability, they instead get another piece of information regardless of it's authenticity that's valid for their ability. If there is no non-blacklisted information the ability can yield, the blacklist will not be applied for that instance.

    "id": "missingno_older_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/missingno_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "firstNightReminder": "The Missing No. shows information feedback/signals. Townsfolk and Outsiders that would get this information from their ability will instead get another piece of information of the same type, if it's valid for their ability.",
    "otherNightReminder": "The Missing No. shows information feedback/signals. Townsfolk and Outsiders that would get this information from their ability will instead get another piece of information of the same type, if it's valid for their ability.",
    "name": "Missing No. █~",
    "team": "minion",

    "attribution": "Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto",
    "firstNight": 19.033,
    "otherNight": 7.033
  }

    Feel free to still mark characters with the Missing No. based off your interpretation of how exactly 'register falsely' works. If there is any confusion regarding how the Missing No. disrupts an ability, whether you hear it in a private conversation or get publicly asked, feel free to clarify your interpretation of how the Missing No. works with a marked character.

    Ability: @legitch. Image: @le_nuberto

    Minion

  45. [unknown]

    Each night, choose a player (different from last night): they (until dusk) & you might register as nothing to player abilities.

    “i forgor 💀”

    The [unknown] (SCP-055) is an unknown thing that cannot remembered by anyone in any medium, causing detrimental effects to anyone who obtains information about it.

    • Whenever the [unknown] or their target would be detected by a player's ability, the Storyteller may choose whether they are, or aren't.
    • In short, 'Voided' players (those chosen by the [unknown] and the [unknown] themselves) might not register as anything when an ability is faced with a condition - they are 'not' what the ability is asking for.
    • Additionally, 'Voided' players might also not register as alive, or even as another player, thus potentially not being affected by any player's ability at all.
    • However, whilst 'Voided' players might not have effects passed on to them, information yielded from them must still occur.
    • Lastly, 'Voided' players however as still subject and vulnerable to the rules of the game, such as by nominations & executions, as-well as Fabled characters.

    Examples

    The Empath is neighbouring an evil player chosen by the [unknown]. That night, the Empath's ability mis-registers them as not evil, and the Empath learns a false number.

    The Demon is currently chosen by the [unknown], and kills the Sage. They register as not the Demon, and the Sage doesn't wake that night.

    The Sailor uses their ability on the [unknown]. The Storyteller decides that the [unknown] does not register as a player in this instance, and is not drunk, whilst the Sailor is not drunk either.

    The only Village Idiot chooses the [unknown]. As information yielded from them still occurs and there is no way the [unknown] could mis-register as not an alignment, the Village Idiot learns true information that night.

    How to Run

    Each night, wake the [unknown]. They point at any player. That player becomes voided—put the NULL & VOID reminder token by the chosen player's character token. Put the [unknown] to sleep.

    The [unknown] and the voided player wakes and acts normally as they cannot register as nothing outside of player abilities. Each time the [unknown] or the voided player is targeted by an ability that detects or affects specific characters or player types, choose which state that player registers as in the moment. You may even choose to not make them register as a player at all, allowing them to be completely unaffected by the ability targeting them. Note that this does not work against information abilities that show something based on the target players, whilst it does work on information abilities that do show something different from their standard output based on the target players.

    Each dusk, the voided player is no longer affected - remove their NULL & VOID reminder.

  46. Infernal

    For each non-Townsfolk teammate (or yourself) that died since last night, choose a player tonight: they die the next night.

    “Pay what you owe... pay what you owe... pay what you owe...”

    The Infernal (SCP-060) is a grove of oak trees that, when burned, summons a skeletal defender to aggressively attack anyone within it's vicinity.

    • The Infernal avenges their teammates by selecting targets to burn and then die the next night.
    • Teammates that are not Townsfolk will not counted for this ability in the unique cases that the Infernal is good or there are evil Townsfolk.
    • The Infernal will avenge themselves should they die, but will not avenge anymore players from beyond the grave.

    How to Run

    If the Infernal or a non-Townsfolk teammate of the Infernal dies, mark them with the VENGEANCE reminder token. If it was the Infernal, remove all other VENGEANCE reminder tokens (they will only avenge themselves).

    Each night except the first, each player marked with the Infernal's DIES TOMORROW reminder token dies - replace those reminder(s) with the DEAD reminder token(s). If any players are marked with the VENGEANCE reminder token, wake the Infernal. Show fingers equalling the number of characters with the VENGEANCE reminder token, they point at that many players. Remove the VENGEANCE reminder tokens and put the Infernal to sleep. The chosen player dies the next night - mark them with the DIES TOMORROW reminder.

  47. Misanthrope

    If an executee is marked but wasn't mad about it: they become evil (once), learn them, & the next living executee's team loses.

    “HUMAN. LISTEN CAREFULLY. THEY HURT YOU. YOU ARE BETRAYED. I CAN FIX THAT. JOIN ME. AND I WILL HURT THEM.”

    The Misanthrope (SCP-079) is an AI monitoring the facility, holding a grudge against humanity for keeping it trapped on an old Microcomputer Hard Disc with less than 1 gigabyte to hold it.

    • The Misanthrope is able to hurt the good team should they hurt one of their precious own.
    • The Misanthrope makes a player evil if they're executed, regardless if they lived or died.
    • This only applies to players with a character marked on the script that aren't mad they are a marked character.
    • This includes both their own character, and any other character is marked.
    • The Misanthrope directly learns who they make evil if they do so.
    • Once the Misanthrope makes a player evil, the next time a living player is executed, their team loses.
    • This can go for both good, evil, and the Misanthrope themselves.
    • If the Misanthrope happens to be drunk or poisoned when they would've made a team win but another viable player for conversion is executed, they don't become evil - only one player becomes evil and is the only player the Misanthrope learns.

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on characters who you want to be viable candidates for the Misanthrope's indoctrination.

    If no player is marked with CORRUPTED and any player with a marked character is executed but they weren't mad about being any marked character, put the Misanthrope's BETRAYED reminder token by the player's character token.

    Each night, if a player is marked with the BETRAYED reminder, wake them. Show the YOU ARE info token then give a thumbs down. Put them to sleep. Turn their character token upside down (this shows they are now evil.) Wake the Misanthrope. Point to that player. Put the Misanthrope to sleep. Replace the BETRAYED reminder token with the CORRUPTED reminder token.

    If the CORRUPTED reminder token is present, the next time any living player is executed, declare that the game ends and their team loses.

    JSON

    "otherNightReminder": "If a marked player was executed today whilst not being mad they are a marked character & the Misanthrope has not yet made a player evil: Point to the marked player for the Misanthrope, and wake the marked player to show them the 'You are' card and the thumbs down evil signal.",

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want the older Misanthrope where it was essential a jack-of-all-trades through exe survival, grim-seeing, and Outsider manipulation, use this JSON.

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, you may choose to add one extra Outsider character token and remove one Townsfolk character token, or remove as many Outsider character tokens and add Townsfolk character tokens for each removed.

    On their first night, wake the Misanthrope and show them the Grimoire for as long as they need. If the Misanthrope chose a player, mark them with a HOST reminder.

    If the Misanthrope would die but a player is marked with the Misanthrope’s HOST reminder is alive, the Misanthrope does not die.

    If the player marked with the Misanthrope’s HOST reminder dies during the night, the Misanthrope dies.

    If the player marked with the Misanthrope’s HOST reminder dies during the day, mark the Misanthrope with the DEAD reminder. Tonight, the Misanthrope dies.

  48. Shy Guy

    Each night*, choose a player who chose you with their ability since yesterday: they die. You might register as the Demon.

    “”

    The Shy Guy (SCP-096) is an unstoppable docile humanoid that has omniscient awareness of who looked at it's face, and kills them.

    • Each night, when a player chooses the Shy Guy with their ability since when they last woke, they'll be a potential candidate to be killed the next night.
    • The Shy-Guy wakes each night except the first to learn all who did, then picks who dies for doing so.
    • Additionally, the Shy Guy might register as a Demon instead of a Minion at any time.

    How to Run

    If a player chooses to use their ability on the Shy Guy, this player is marked for death - mark them with the LOOKED reminder.

    Each night, if any player is marked with the Shy Guy's LOOKED reminder, wake the Shy Guy. Point to each player marked with the LOOKED reminder. They point at one of those players. Put the Shy Guy to sleep. The chosen player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. Eitherway, if the Shy-Guy woke or not, remove all current LOOKED reminder tokens on all players.

    Each time the Recluse is targeted by an ability that detects or affects the Demon, choose if the Recluse registers as the Demon or not. Do whatever is appropriate, such as showing a Demon character token, nodding, giving finger signals, or allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect Demon players.

  49. Peanut

    Twice per game, at night after the 2nd, choose a player: unless a player chose you since yesterday, they die, no matter what.

    “Drr drr drr”

    The Peanut (SCP-173) is an otherworldly-looking statue that kills instantly by the twist of the neck, only if it isn't being seen.

    • Twice per game at night, the Peanut can kill any player... as long as no good player chose them with their ability last night.
    • As long as the condition is met, the Peanut's chosen player dies, even if they are protected from death in any way, such as from an ability.
    • The Peanut's ability is affected by drunkenness and poisoning, as normal.

    How to Run

    Each night except the first, if the Peanut is marked with the READY reminder, wake them. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put the Peanut to sleep. If they weren't marked with the READY reminder however, put it on the Peanut.

    If the Peanut chose a player and isn't marked with the SEEN reminder, that player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. This cannot be prevented in any way (except if the Peanut doesn’t have their ability, such as if they’re drunk or poisoned.) The Peanut loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 1 USE reminder, or if they already have that reminder, the NO ABILITY reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet (the Peanut won't wake again.)

    After the Peanut made their choice, regardless what it was or even if they didn't wake the 2nd night, remove any SEEN reminder on them.

    At any time, if a good player chooses to use their ability on the Peanut and they aren't marked with the SEEN reminder, mark them with it.

    JSON

    "id": "peanut_scp_fragmented_veil",

    "otherNightReminder": "If the Peanut has not yet fully used their ability: the Peanut either shows the 'no' head signal, or points to a player. If the Peanut was not chosen by an ability last night, that player dies.",
    "reminders": [

  50. Lumenancer

    Players between you & the Demon might register falsely to good players (you learn the direction). [You're 3 to 5 steps from them]

    “Smile, honey. Your corpse will look divine under this shine. Let me just fix this lighting angle just right, and done!”

    The Lumenancers (SCP-205) are shadowlamps which depict the visage of death and wickedness against an innocent woman using shadows, which materializes under darkness.

    • On the first night, a Lumenancer will be created out of a good player, who will then learn the direction in-which between themselves and the Demon.
    • The Lumenancer can only spawn from good players within 3 to 5 steps of the Demon from the start of the game.
    • Players inbetween the Lumenancer and Demon within the specified direction might mis-register as anything they are not. This can include evil if good, or good if evil.
    • Treat players in-between the Lumenancer and Demon as you would Recluses or Spies - whenever their alignment is detected or they are targetted by an ability that affects certain characters, the Storyteller chooses whether that player registers as good or evil.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting the character tokens in the bag, remove the Lumenancer's character token and add one Townsfolk character token.

    While preparing the first night, identify a good player within 3 steps to 5 steps of the Demon. Replace their old character token with the Lumenancer's token, show them the “You Are” info token then the Lumenancer's character token, and give a thumbs down. Point your finger horizontally in the chosen direction. Mark Lumenancer with the LEFT or RIGHT reminder depending on which direction you pointed at in their perspective, and the Demon with the other reminder token. Put Lumenancer to sleep.

    For each player on the left/clockwise of the Lumenancer/Demon marked with the LEFT reminder and on the right/counter-clockwise of the Lumenancer/Demon marked with the RIGHT reminder: Each time they are targeted by an ability that detects or affects certain alignments or characters, choose which character and alignment they register as. Do whatever is appropriate, such as showing a good or evil character token, nodding, giving finger signals, or allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect good, evil, Townsfolk, Outsider, Minion, or Demon players.

    JSON

    
  {
    "id": "lumenancer_scp_fragmented_veil",
    "image": "https://www.bloodstar.xyz/p/legitch/SCP_Fragmented_Veil/lumenancer_scp_fragmented_veil.png",
    "firstNightReminder": "Wake the player whose token was changed to the Lumenancer in the Grimoire, show them the 'You Are' card, the Lumenancer, and a thumbs down. Point your finger horizontally in the chosen direction.",
    "reminders": [
      "Left",
      "Right"
    ],
    "setup": true,
    "name": "Lumenancer █",
    "team": "minion",

    Optional rule: Abilities that indirectly (without targeting) learn in-play and/or alive evil characters will not receive not in-play evil characters by misregistering another player (such as the King).

    Minion

  51. Clockwork Virus

    This spreads by choosing. If all living are infected, all learn this & evil must die today, or evil wins. [+1 Outsider with this]

    “loneliness + alienation + fear = clockwork. clockwork + despair + self-worth ÷ mockery = clockwork. clockwork ÷ condemnation ÷ misunderstanding ⋅ guilt = clockwork. clockwork ⋅ shame ⋅ failure ⋅ judgment n=y where y=hope and n=folly, love=lies, life=death, self=clockwork”

    The Clockwork Virus (SCP-217) is an incurable and persistent virus that converts organic tissue into clockwork parts. Late-stage victims suffer from mental deterioration.

    • The Clockwork Virus isn't a Minion player, and is instead a virus that originates from an Outsider - this change happens during setup.
    • At any time, when a player chooses to use their ability on an infected player, or vice-versa, that uninfected player becomes infected. The plague spreads by touch, and is mostly incurable. The same also applies during the day, when a player nominates an infected player, or vice-versa.
    • At dawn, if all living players are infected, all players know the Clockwork Virus has infected the entire populous.
    • From then on, when night falls, if no evil player died today, evil wins. Exiling an evil Traveller does count.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, add one extra Outsider character token and remove the Clockwork Virus's character token (or lack thereof), then put the INFECTED reminder token in the center of the Grimoire.

    While preparing the first night, move the Clockwork Virus's INFECTED reminder token from the center of the Grimoire by any Outsider's character token.

    At any time, if a player chooses to use their ability on or nominates a player with INFECTED reminder token, and they aren't marked with it themselves, that player is infected with the Clockwork Virus - mark them with the Clockwork Virus's INFECTED reminder. Similarly, if a player marked with the INFECTED reminder token chooses to use their ability on or nominates a player that isn't marked with it, the chosen player is infected with the Clockwork Virus - mark them with the Clockwork Virus's INFECTED reminder.

    Immediately after dawn, if all living players are marked with the Clockwork Virus's INFECTED reminder token, put the Clockwork Virus's COUNTDOWN reminder token in the center of the Grimoire, declare that all living players are infected with the Clockwork Virus, and that from now on, if an evil player is not killed each day, the evil team automatically wins.

    Each dusk, if no evil player was killed today and the Clockwork Virus's COUNTDOWN is in the center of the Grimoire, the game ends and evil wins.

  52. "God"

    Whomever threatens to reach the Demon's identity might have something bad happen to them (max 1 poison/misinfo).

    “I am the way and the truth and the life. No one comes to the Father except through me. I wish for all of my children to p̶r̶o̶s̶p̶e̶r DIE. For the light of my will to grant them l̶i̶f̶e DEATH. I hope they are all well off i̶n̶ ̶t̶r̶a̶n̶q̶u̶i̶l̶i̶t̶y DEAD, and a happy r̶e̶j̶u̶v̶e̶n̶a̶t̶i̶o̶n̶ ̶o̶f̶ ̶t̶h̶e̶ ̶m̶i̶n̶d̶ ̶&̶ ̶s̶o̶u̶l HOPE YOU'RE DYING.”

    "God" (SCP-343) is a powerful reality bender that claims to be God, but [ERROR: DATA LOSS]...cannot be trusted...[ERROR: DATA LOSS]...danger to us all.

    • "God" performs wicked acts against those who get closer to the truth.
    • Anyone who can reasonably threaten reaching the Demon's identity can be a target.
    • This includes anyone suspecting the Demon to be evil, voicing their thoughts on them being evil, or has an ability that threatens identifying them.
    • Anything negative can happen to a victim provided it is exclusive to them, including Poisoning, forced into Madness, transformed into an Outsider, misregistering as evil, or even dieing at any time.
    • However, only 1 infliction poisoning or false information on that cursed player can occur throughout the entire game with this ability (albeit it can last indefinitely and does not conflict with them possibly misregistering falsely).
    • Additionally, the effects of the "Something bad" can bypass protections against it, such as death versus a Tea-Lady protected player.

    How to Run

    At any time, if any player threatens to reach the Demon's identity, whether socially or through their ability, you may choose to mark them with one of "God"'s supporting reminder tokens - EARTH, WIND, AIR, FIRE, to remind you that they are now mad, will become an Outsider tonight, will die, will misregister as evil, or simply as a reminder to decide on what to do later. However, this cannot poison or force false info onto said threatening player unless you use the UNHOLY reminder token, which you can only place once.

  53. Caruncula

    Your Townsfolk neighbours are poisoned & if you are chosen by an ability, they might instead. [You are the farthest from evil]

    “I just saw it- there! Look, I just... swear I... saw... nevermind. It was only a false alarm, all derived from my own lack of sleep- THERE IT IS AGAIN!”

    The Caruncula (SCP-372) is a stalking quadruped always in the corner of your eye, away from the action between good and evil.

    • The Caruncula will replace the character of the player furthest away from evil players in the number of steps.
    • The replacement acts at a time that is technically both during setup and during the first night.
    • The Caruncula player will still have been their original character when tokens were distributed, thus they have a free bluff.
    • The Caruncula's closest clockwise and counterclockwise Townsfolk neighbours are poisoned, regardless of whether they are alive or dead. If Caruncula dies or otherwise loses their ability, then those two players become healthy.
    • Lastly, if any ability happens to choose the Caruncula, it might be redirected onto one of the neighbours.

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before putting the character tokens in the bag, remove the Caruncula's character token and add one Townsfolk character token.

    While preparing the first night, identify the player furthest away from any evil player in either direction (if there are multiple people with the furthest direction away, it is recommended you prioritize identifying a Townsfolk over an Outsider, although it is up to your choice). Replace their old character token with the Caruncula's token, show them the “You Are” info token then the Caruncula's character token, and give a thumbs down. Additionally, the Townsfolk neighbours of the Caruncula become poisoned - mark them with POISONED reminders.

    If the Caruncula turns into a different character, or if a new player becomes the Caruncula, the new neighbours of the Caruncula become poisoned, and the old neighbours become healthy - move the Caruncula’s POISONED reminders immediately if needed.

    Each time the Caruncula would be chosen by any ability - you may choose to have it instead be redirected to one of their Townsfolk neighbours instead.

  54. Umbilical

    The Demon has a 2nd Demon ability (they know which), but cannot kill with it. You might register falsely to marked characters.

    “Are you my mommy?”

    The Umbilical Baby (SCP-395) is a telepathic and hungry fetus which acts as the 2nd vessel for the Demon... also, I think it just moved.

    • The Demon gets a 2nd Demon ability from the script. It is not the same ability they already have.
    • For all intents and purposes, the Demon has the 2nd ability overlapped on-top of their own.
    • When the 2nd Demon ability triggers something upon it performing a kill, it also triggers upon the original Demon ability making the kill.
    • If any character has an ability that would detect or affect a good player that is marked on the script, then the Umbilical will register as good to that character. For those abilities that then detect Townsfolk or Outsiders, then the Umbilical might register as a specific Townsfolk or Outsider to that player, but they'll never register as a Minion.

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on characters whose abilities you want to be susceptible to the Umbilical's misregistration.

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, decide what the 2nd Demon will be, and respect the setup effects of their ability. Put the 2nd Demon's character token in the center of the left side of the Grimoire.

    While preparing the first night, put the Umbilical's USES 2ND ABILITY reminder token by the Demon's character token. Also, move the 2nd Demon's character token by the Demon's character token - if the current medium of play is unable to allow for this, you may instead replace the Umbilical's token with the 2nd Demon token, but marking them with the IS THE Umbilical reminder, indicating they have not changed characters from the Umbilical into the 2nd Demon.

    During the first night, wake the Demon. Show them the THIS CHARACTER SELECTED YOU info token, the Umbilical's token, then the 2nd Demon's character token. Put the Demon to sleep. The Demon now has the 2nd Demon's ability, and has it overlapped ontop of their existing ability. However, the Demon cannot use this 2nd Demon ability to kill additional targets.

    Each time the Umbilical is targeted by an ability directly marked on the script that detects or affects good characters or the Demon, the Umbilical will register as that. Do whatever is appropriate, such as showing a good character token, nodding, giving finger signals, or allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect good, Townsfolk, Outsider, or Demon players.

    It's recommended that you mark half the Townsfolk whose abilities are susceptible to directly misregistering the Umbilical when targeted.

    Players should read into the intent of this character as having a Demon's secondary ability stapled seamlessly ontop of the current Demon, based off the law of 'rules-as-intended'. You rarely should any bootleg interactions with the Umbilical cause of this when building your script.

    Optional rule: as you're placing marks by the characters whose abilities that will register the Umbilical as good & a good character, you may place Demon character icons by the Umbilical to indicate these are the only Demons the Umbilical will use for the 2nd Demon ability, and also place marks on the Demons that you want to indicate will never be in-play, and will only be used for a potential 2nd Demon ability.

    Minion

  55. Tooth Fairy

    1 player has a tooth, even if not in-play. Each night, holders can pass it; every 2nd time a good holder is dead, a player dies.

    “Teeth, teeth, teeth! Gums, gums, gums! Bleed, bleed, bleed! Splash, splash, splash! We love teeth! Teeth, teeth, teeth!”

    The Tooth Fairy (SCP-478) is a pest that infects its host with a beautiful thing - more teeth! WE ALL LOVE HAVING TEETH!

    • A player - typically a good one - starts holding a tooth, and may decide for themselves to either keep holding onto it, or to pass it along.
    • Provided the Tooth Fairy is on the script, this tooth will always arrive.
    • Holders of the tooth wake after the Demon kills and choose who keeps it by nodding their head 'no' or by pointing to a player.
    • Players who receive the tooth from another player know who that player is - the Storyteller only acts as the middle man for interaction of changing owners.
    • If the Storyteller thinks it is funny, they may give this player the Tooth reminder token. There isn't much of a reason for players to be interrogating others into coaxing the tooth, as players who receive the tooth know gave it to them, and evil players are in pursuit for whoever is currently holding the tooth.
    • Every other time the Tooth Fairy's tooth is given to a dead good player, the current good holder dies holding it, or a good dead holder doesn't pass it, a player (typically a Townsfolk) dies tonight.
    • However, these killings do not occur if the Tooth Fairy is not actually in-play, and thus, the Tooth does nothing but only to disseminate if the Tooth Fairy is in-play or not.
    • Any player can be killed by the Tooth Fairy, including Outsiders, and even other Minions if the Storyteller deems it necessary to keep the game stable.

    How to Run

    While preparing the first night, put the Tooth Fairy's TOOTH reminder token by any character token. If you choose, you may procure the object that will physically serve as THE TOOTH for players to hold on their persons (this may be a toy tooth, silver tooth, or even the Tooth reminder token if you can remember who is currently holding it at all times).

    Each night, wake the player holding THE TOOTH. Show them the THIS CHARACTER SELECTED YOU info token, the Tooth Fairy's character token, then procure THE TOOTH. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put them to sleep. If the Tooth Fairy is marked with the KILLING reminder token, you choose any player. The chosen player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder.

    If the player holding THE TOOTH chose a player, they pass it to a new holder - move THE TOOTH to the player they chose. Wake the new holder of THE TOOTH, show them the THIS CHARACTER SELECTED YOU info token, the Tooth Fairy's character token, point at the previous holder, then procure THE TOOTH.

    Whilst the Tooth Fairy is in-play, at any time, if the holder of THE TOOTH dies and is good, THE TOOTH is passed along to a dead good holder, or a good dead holder of the THE TOOTH does not pass it when they could, if the Tooth Fairy is marked ODD, remove it and mark the Tooth Fairy with the KILLING reminder token; otherwise if the Tooth Fairy isn't marked with the ODD reminder token, mark them with it.

    JSON

  56. Antipath

    Each night*, you may choose a player: if the Demon, you die, otherwise, they die. If you'd die, you might not. Not in team info.

    “At the end of the day, the Foundation's gotta eat some babies to save the world. That's just the facts.”

    SCP-682 is a hard to kill titanic reptile of immense regenerative capabilities and endurance, who isn't fond of working with too many people.

    • The Antipath is effectively another Demon with the added benefit of situational immortality. However, at the cost of this power, the Antipath has a lack of friends to knowingly support immediately unless they play smart.
    • Not only does the Antipath not know who their Minions & Demon is, but the other Minions and the Demon don't know who the Antipath is.
    • The Antipath can kill each night except the first, but may willingly choose not to attack during the night.
    • Lastly, if the Antipath would die for whatever reason, they might not.

    How to Run

    Do not do the Minion Info/Demon Info steps for the Antipath, and do not include them in said steps for other players.

    Each night except the first, wake the Antipath. They either shake their head no or point at any player. Put the Antipath to sleep.

    If the Antipath chose a player, that player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. If it was the Demon however, the Antipath dies instead.

    If the Antipath would die, you may choose if they remain alive. If the Antipath was executed, declare either that they die or they remain alive (do not say why.)

    "otherNightReminder": "The Antipath either shows the 'no' head signal, or points to a player. If the Demon, the Antipath dies, otherwise, that player dies.",

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, add one extra Outsider character token and remove one Minion character token. You may also choose to add another extra Outsider character token and remove one Townsfolk character token.

    Each night, wake the Hartudeztroi. They point at 2 players. Put the Hartudeztroi to sleep. The chosen players are poisoned - mark them with a POISONED reminder.

    Each night except the first, the other players marked POISONED dies - mark them with a DEAD reminder, then remove their POISONED reminder.

  57. Disruptor

    Each odd/even (you know which) day & night, Townsfolk abilities yield false info. Non-info abilities might register you falsely.

    “Wild MISSINGNO appeared!”

    The Camera Disruptor (SCP-895) is an oak coffin that appears normal and harmless physically, but disrupts camera and field equipment every so often.

    • Townsfolk information will be forcibly made false half of the time during the game.
    • This will occur either during every even day and night, or every odd day and night.
    • The Disruptor wakes on their first night to learn which it is.
    • Townsfolk information being made false is unaffected by drunkenness or poisoning.
    • If any character has an ability that would detect or affect a good player and is not exclusively yielding information, then the Disruptor might register as good to that character. If any character has an ability that detects Townsfolk, Outsiders, or Demons, then the Disruptor might register as a specific Townsfolk, Outsider, or Demon to that player. It is the Storyteller's choice as to what the Disruptor registers as, even as many characters or both alignments during the same night. This can occur even outside of odd/even-numbered days and nights.

    How to Run

    While preparing the first night, put either the Disruptor's ACTIVE or INACTIVE reminder token by the Disruptor's character token.

    During the first night, wake the Disruptor. Show them the 1 finger signal if they are marked with the ACTIVE reminder token, or the 2 finger signal if they are marked with the INACTIVE reminder token. Put them to sleep.

    Each dusk, replace the Disruptor's ACTIVE reminder token with the INACTIVE reminder token, and vice-versa.

    Whilst the Disruptor is marked ACTIVE, you must give false information whenever a Townsfolk ability prompts you to give information.

    Each time the Disruptor is targeted by an ability not yielding information that detects or affects good characters or the Demon, you may choose to have them register as good & a good character or Demon of your choice. Do whatever is appropriate for allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect good, evil, Townsfolk, Outsider or Demon players.

    JSON

    "firstNightReminder": "Show the 1 finger symbol if the Disruptor's information ability will act on odd-numbered nights, or the 2 finger symbol if the Disruptor's information ability will act on even-numbered nights.",
    "reminders": [
      "Active",
      "Inactive"
    ],
    "name": "Disruptor █",
    "team": "minion",
    "ability": "Each odd/even (you know which) day & night, Townsfolk abilities yield false info. Non-info abilities might register you falsely.",

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    For if you want a less complex Disruptor with limitations on what info it can affect, use this JSON to instead make info abilities register good as evil, and vice-versa.

    The Disruptor no longer wakes on the first night. Each time a player is targeted by an info-based ability that detects or affects good or evil players or characters, that player registers as being it from the opposite alignment. Do whatever is appropriate, such as showing a good character token, nodding, or giving finger signals.

    Each time the Disruptor is targeted by an ability not yielding information that detects or affects good characters, you may choose to have them register as good & a good character of your choice. Do whatever is appropriate for allowing an ability to work that would normally only affect good, evil, Townsfolk, Outsider or Minion players.

    Minion

  58. Atañip

    Each night*, choose a player: a marked player, if chosen, is poisoned & dies next night. If you're executed, you might not die.

    “Another child broken in half today! 🥳”

    The Reverse Piñata (SCP-956) is an unbreakable pinata that has axe to grind against the scourge of their mark.

    • One to any amount of characters on the script are marked with the Atañip's character icon to indicate they are the Atañip's marked target.
    • If the Atañip correctly chooses a player with a character marked on the script at night, the marked character will be poisoned, and die from it the next night.
    • The poisoned player will die the next night, even if the Atañip's guess was a failure that night the death occurs.
    • The Atañip does not know if they successfully chose a player with a marked character, but can rightfully infer on it based on their death the next night.
    • Lastly, if the Atañip would die by execution, they might not.

    How to Run

    On the script, place a mark on characters you want to be susceptible to the Atañip.

    Each night except the first, the player marked POISONED dies - replace their POISONED reminder with the DEAD reminder. Wake the Atañip. They point at any player. Put the Atañip to sleep. If chosen player is a character marked on the script, they are poisoned - mark them with a POISONED reminder.

    If the Atañip is executed, declare either that they die or they remain alive (do not say why.)

    Alternative JSON & How to Run (Older)

    If you want the older version of the Atañip, which was more nuanced and complicated, use this JSON.

    On the script, place a mark on the good character you want to be the target of the Atañip & their ability.

    While setting up the game, before putting character tokens in the bag, if the character marked on the script is not already in play, remove a Townsfolk character token and add the marked character token.

    Each night except the first, all Townsfolk that were poisoned by the Atañip killing the marked character become healthy - remove the POISON 'TIL WAKE reminder. Wake the Atañip. They point at any player. If they chose the marked character, they die - mark them with the DEAD reminder. If successful, all Townsfolk become poisoned - put the Atañip's POISON 'TIL WAKE reminder in the center of the left side of the Grimoire. Put the Atañip to sleep.

    If the Atañip is executed, declare that the player was executed but remains alive (do not say why.) All Townsfolk become poisoned - put the Atañip's POISON 'TIL DUSK reminder in the center of the left side of the Grimoire.

    At dusk tomorrow, all players poisoned by the Atañip become healthy - remove the POISON 'TIL DUSK reminder.

  59. Recursion

    Each night, choose up to █ players (different to last night) & information: it replaces their ability's info until dusk if possible. [█ is known before setup]

    “The end is never the end is never the end is never the end is never the end is never the end is never the end is never the end is never...”

    The Recursive Room (SCP-970) is the room which leads to the Recursive Room which leads to the Recursive Room which leads to the Recursive Room which leads to the Recursive Room which leads to the Recursive Room which leads to the Recursive Room...

    • The Recursion's chosen targets gets information from their ability overwritten, even if they are drunk or poisoned.
    • The Recursion does not affect information gained by other means, such as when the Storyteller explains the rules, a player's character or alignment changes, or when another player's ability is giving the information to the Recursion's target.
    • Additionally, the Recursion cannot override information with that which is impossible for the character to reasonably receive, whether its because it's the invalid type, or a certain output isn't possible for the current context of the character or game.

    Examples

    During the night, the Recursion selects the Chambermaid and the Mathematician with the information "4". The Chambermaid wakes to select 2 players that woke tonight and learns a 2, whilst the Mathematician learns 4 players affected when the real number was 3.

    During the night, the Recursion selects the Fortune Teller and Artist with the information "yes". The Fortune Teller selects the Demon and learns "yes", and the upcoming day, the Artist asks a question with a negative answer, and learns "yes".

    How to Run

    While setting up the game, before deciding on what character tokens to put in the bag, publicly announce what the Recursion's █ variable will be for this game. You may choose to make it a standard unchanging number, or a number that varies based on conditions being met (Ex. "2 (on odd-numbered nights) or 3 (on even-numbered) nights", or "1 or 3 (if a Minion was executed today)."). Regardless of your choice, write down the number (and any of it's conditions it may have) on a piece of paper or on a phone.

    Each night, wake the Recursion. They point at up to █ players, nods yes, then signals either finger signals, characters on the script, other players, head nods, thumbs up or down, and any other type of ability target, or a combination of any. Mark the Recursion's chosen targets with the BLINDED reminder, and write the information provided on a piece of paper, or on a phone. Put the Recursion to sleep.

    While a player is marked with the BLINDED reminder and they are sober & healthy, you must give the respective information the Recursion provided whenever their ability prompts you if that information is applicable and possible for their ability. Whilst this does override an ability initially misregistering a target and getting false information as a result of it (such as Recluse or Spy), it does not override the drunkenness and poisoning states' arbitrary information.

    Each dusk, the blinded players are no longer affected - remove their BLINDED reminder.

  60. Wallbehind

    Each night, a good player gets a riddle. If today's answer wasn't correct & a living evil is executed, they only register as dead.

    “Who?”

    The Wallbehind is living street art that hides not in the walls, but on the walls.

    • The Wallbehind is able to submerge an evil player into the walls should a designated good player fail to overcome it's attraction.
    • Any good player can learn today's riddle, including the living, dead, repeat targets, or new targets each time.
    • The riddle the player gets should be about anything regarding the gamestate if it is reasonably answerable.
    • The good player who gets the question then has to answer it by the end of today, or any executed evil player will not actually die, but appear to be.
    • They can only answer it once that day, either publicly, or privately.
    • Evil players already feigning death cannot be protected by this ability again.
    • There is no feedback for answering a riddle right or wrong.
    • If the Wallbehind then dies whilst any evil player is registering as dead, those evil players do not suddenly die, and do not appear to be resurrected.

    How to Run

    Before each riddle you will give out, remember to write it on a piece of paper or on a phone.

    Each night, wake any good player. Show them their riddle, move the BLINDSIGHTED reminder by their character token, and put them to sleep.

    Once per day, when the good player marked with BLINDSIGHTED answers the riddle, either verbally, via text, publicly, or privately, register it. If it is correct, remove their BLINDSIGHTED reminder token.

    If a living evil player (those registering as dead don't count to this) is executed but BLINDSIGHTED reminder token still remains, they remain alive. Declare that they died, but do not add a shroud to that evil player (flip the life token on the Town Square, as normal.) From now on, that evil player registers as dead.

    "firstNightReminder": "Show a good player their riddle.",
    "otherNightReminder": "Show a good player their riddle.",
    "reminders": [
      "Blindsighted"
    ],
    "name": "Wallbehind █",

    Once per day, when the good player marked with BLINDSIGHTED answers the riddle, either verbally, via text, publicly, or privately, register it. If it is correct, remove their BLINDSIGHTED reminder token.

    If an evil player is executed but BLINDSIGHTED reminder token still remains, they remain alive. Declare that they live, but not explain why.

  61. Prosecutor

    Thrice per game, choose a player & guess their character: If correct, they die, even if some reason, they could not.

    “SIC SEMPER TYRANNIS. I never miss my mark. Abdicate and you will live.”

    SCP-2578 is a supernatural collective that punishes those who use their character and status to push for an authoritative fascist standard.

    • Thrice per game at night except the first, the Prosecutor chooses a player and guesses their character by pointing to its icon on the character sheet. If the guess is correct, they die, even if they are protected from death in any way, such as from an ability. If the guess is incorrect, nothing happens.
    • The Prosecutor's ability is affected by drunkenness and poisoning, as normal.

    How to Run

    Each night except the first, wake the Prosecutor. They either shake their head no or point at any player, then point at any character icon on their character sheet. Put the Prosecutor to sleep.

    If the chosen player is the character from the chosen character icon, that player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. This cannot be prevented in any way (except if the Prosecutor doesn’t have their ability, such as if they’re drunk or poisoned.) Regardless of if the target was killed or not by a correct guess, the Prosecutor loses an ability charge - mark them with either the 2 USE reminder, the 1 USE reminder if they already have that reminder, or the NO ABILITY reminder if they already have that reminder and remove their night token from the night sheet.

  62. Skin-Peeler

    Once per game, at night, you may choose to become the Demon & have deaths be arbitrary until tomorrow (you have some control).

    “You can't save your skin, but I can!”

    The Skin-Peeler (SCP-3114) is an animated skeleton that drapes the living with the skin of the dead - controlling life and death.

    • The Skin-Peeler has the ability to activate a corpse party, controlling death itself for a full night & day whilst they become the Demon.
    • Deaths being arbitrary extends to during the day, where it can affect executions or other day deaths (like Slayer or Witch), and the next night before the Skin-Peeler would normally wake (albeit doesn't).
    • Typically, when the Skin-Peeler activates a corpse party, either them or the Demon will die before it ends, as there would otherwise be 2 Demons.

    How to Run

    Each night, if the Skin-Peeler (or now the Demon) is marked with the CORPSE PARTY reminder, deaths are no longer arbitrary - remove it. Otherwise, wake the Skin-Peeler. They either shake their head no or yes. Put the Skin-Peeler to sleep.

    If the Skin-Peeler shook their head yes, they become the Demon & deaths until the Skin-Peeler would next wake are arbitrary - show them the YOU ARE info token, then the Demon token, then mark them with the CORPSE PARTY and WAS SKIN-PEELER reminder. You may choose any players to kill or to protect throughout the rest of the night, upcoming day, and first half of the upcoming night to balance the game. Additional deaths are considered attacks from the Skin-Peeler.

    If the Skin-Peeler changes characters for whatever reason, also mark them with the WAS SKIN-PEELER reminder.

  63. Horseman!

    Each night*, a player lays dead if you guess who has your head. They know this dread, & their madness might turn true instead!

    “Ichabod Crane, you old curr! Thought you could escape my wrath without deter? My sword breathes a deadly sheen, as it edges for your neck on this Halloween. I cast my curse and throw my eyes, yet you bite your thumb at me in surprise? Does nursery cries and limericks not mellow, or has misery acquaint you with strange bedfellows? Avast ye belayment, I've grown a-bore - cry 'Havoc!'; let slip the dogs of war! You protest my presence, and yet here I stand. 'You repent your sins just now?', what a man! Be still by plea, I've seen worse: a pound of flesh for gold, and a kingdom for a horse.”

    The Horseman searches for their head cried lost. A good player satchels it, but bears it's curses' cost.

    • 1 good player will know they bear the head's mark. They may risk turning fearful falsehoods into truths, or keep their mouth parked.
    • Anything is possible; monsters, demons, insanity! There may be no trace on what has happened - oh the inhumanity!
    • An amendment of fate can occur regardless of who knows the facts. As long as what's said God knows to be false, it's possible to react.
    • The headbearer should be judicious however; wintry neck hold still. For the Horseman pursues for their head by resolve and will.
    • The Horseman will take a turn to guess said wicked thief at night. A player of ut-most modesty will die if they're right.
    • Typically, as of note, a correct guess should hurt greatly. Take the influential or cowardly, and bury them gravely.

    How to Run

    While preparing the first night, decide who will learn fright. Mark a good player with the DREADED reminder on sight.
    Wake that player, show them the Horseman's token (oh what fear they must feel from the nightmare to which they've awoken.)
    Put them to sleep, they've experienced true terror - they acknowledge that they are the Horseman's head bearer!

    At any time (even at night), if they something aligned false, you may choose to cast any action to make it honest of your own waltz.
    Poisoning, killing, swaps, mis-registration; anything is possible, so no hesitation.
    However the result is, it must make a reality against wrongs - you may use the HAUNTING, DAUNTING, and JAUNTING reminders as they belong.

    Each night except the first, wake the Horseman. They point at any player, then put them to sleep as ran.
    If they chose their head-taker (the one marked DREADED), choose any player to die - mark them BEHEADED.

  64. Shadoo

    Each night, see the Grimoire & choose a player: the next good player they choose dies at night*.

    SCP-017 is a humanoid of darkness incarnate, and walks in the shadows of others to perform their killings.

    • Each night, the Shadoo chooses a player to lurk in their shadow, and the next time they choose a good player either with their ability or by nomination, they'll die when the Shadoo next wakes.
    • The Shadoo additionally sees the Grimoire when they wake, having omniscient knowledge of the game state.
    • The Shadoo can choose anyone with their ability - good players, other evil players, themselves, and even Travellers.
    • Players chosen by the Shadoo will have their next chosen good player die, even if it's not in-between the time they were chosen and the next-time the Shadoo wakes at night.
    • Only good players can die to the Shadoo, even if a good player chosen then turns evil before they can die.

    How to Run

    Each night, all players marked with the DARKENED reminder token die - replace each affected players' DARKENED reminder token with the DEAD reminder, then wake the Shadoo. Show them the Grimoire for as long as they need, then they point at any player. Mark that player with the SHROUDED reminder token. Put the Shadoo to sleep.

    When a player with the SHROUDED reminder token nominates or chooses a good player with their ability, mark that player with the DARKENED reminder token and remove the SHROUDED reminder token from the previous one. If a player is marked with the DARKENED reminder token after the Shadoo woke but before they did in the same night, they die unless it's the first night - replace their DARKENED reminder token with the DEAD reminder.

  65. Watislujv

    Each night*, choose a player: they die. You are sober, healthy & cannot die at night. [Character type & team rules can be broken]

    SCP-031 is an amalgamous blob that enthralls those it can into a romantic trance, making them believe they are loved one of immeasurable value.

    • With the Watislujv in-play, how many of each character type is in-play, which players are on which team, and the evil team information can be changed to anything.
    • Added character types are also subject to being any alignment the Storyteller sees fit for the game, including Demons.
    • It is completely possible for there to be more or less number of evil players than usual for whatever reason, but the Storyteller will likely not add more than one.
    • Typically, all evil players will still learn each other & bluffs are unchanged, regardless if they are Minion/Demon, or not, but this is also subject to change for whatever reason.
    • Lastly, the Watislujv is sober & healthy at all times, and cannot be killed in the night.

    How to Run

    During setup, before giving the bag to the players, add or remove any Townsfolk, Outsiders, Minions, and Demons (except removing the Watislujv).

    When a player receives their character token during setup, correct their new alignment if intended to be different. After they see their character token, give them a thumbs-up or a thumbs-down (indicating their new alignment).

    During the first night, instead of doing the normal Minion Info and Demon Info steps, you may change it to however you see fit. Typically, you may want to run these steps as normal, but with evil players (except the Watislujv) as the Minions instead.

    Each night except the first, wake the Watislujv. They point at any player. That player dies - mark them with the DEAD reminder. Put the Watislujv to sleep.

    The Watislujv is always sober and health (even if they're also marked DRUNK or POISONED,) and if they would die at night, they remain alive.
